## SECTION 4: DATA FLOW

This section follows the data of project A (`goyal-sidhant/AnnualStatement_oldv1`, branch `main`, snapshot `bf2f1dd`, 27-07-2026) from the moment it enters the program to the moment it leaves it as a file, a screen or a log line. Every arrow names the `file:line` that performs the step. Feature IDs (`A-F…`) refer to Section 3, rule IDs (`A-R…`) to Section 5.

### 4.0 How to read this section

- **READ** = established by reading the code. **RAN** = executed on made-up inputs in a scratch copy of the program (never in the repository). "RAN (me)" = run for this section: scripts `/root/merge-scratch/run/A-S45/scripts/s1_end_to_end.py` (a full pass through scan → organise → reports → Step 4 scan → Step 4 naming → consolidated workbook, with every Excel-only call replaced by a clearly named stand-in), `s2_rule_values.py` and `s3_more_values.py` (exact values of selected rules) and `s4_cache_shapes.py` (the settings file with odd contents, under Xvfb); their printed output is saved in `/root/merge-scratch/run/A-S45/outputs/`. "RAN VERIFY-A-S45" = run by the independent verification pass of this section, in its own scratch copy: scripts `/root/merge-scratch/run/VERIFY-A-S45/scripts/` (`s1_end_to_end.py` to `s4_cache_shapes.py` re-run with identical results apart from file sizes and clock times, plus `v_patterns.py`, `v_keys.py`, `v_abbrev_merge.py`, `v_consolidator.py`, `v_validate.py`). "RAN (TRACE)", "RAN (EXT)", "RAN (CORE)", "RAN (PQ)" = runs made by the working-note readers (`A_live_traces.md`, `A_outside_files_state.md`, `A_code_core_utils.md`, `A_code_pq_extractor.md`). Everything that drives Microsoft Excel through COM (Windows' way for one program to remote-control another) cannot run on this Linux machine and is always READ.
- **HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW** = confidence in an inference that goes beyond what was literally read or printed.
- **[GST RULE]** marks a step that decides which return, table, sheet or cell a figure or file belongs to, a state code, a period, and similar. This section only says what the code does with such data; Section 5 lists the rules themselves. Nothing here judges whether a GST rule is right.
- Made-up names only: `Alpha Test Traders`, `Beta Demo Industries Private Limited`, `<Client A>` and similar are invented.

**Plain-English terms used in this section** (each explained once):

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| in memory | held only while the program runs; lost when it closes |
| record / dictionary | a small table of named values that the program passes around, e.g. one file's name, size, type and client |
| key | the label used to find a record, e.g. the client key `Alpha Test Traders-MH` |
| Tk variable | a value attached to a box or button on the window (Tk is Python's built-in window toolkit) |
| worker thread | a second line of work running beside the window so the window does not freeze |
| openpyxl | a Python library that writes `.xlsx` files without Excel; when it saves a workbook it drops Power Query |
| Power Query | Excel's data-import tool; its queries live inside the workbook and are re-run by "refresh" |
| COM / pywin32 | Windows' remote-control mechanism, and the Python package that provides it |
| Links sheet | the sheet in the ITC and Sales templates into which the program writes folder paths and file names |
| staging file | a temporary local copy that is worked on and then copied to its final place |
| glob | a wildcard file search such as `ITC_Report_*.xlsx` |
| mtime / date modified | the time a file or folder was last changed; a folder's mtime changes when something is added, removed or renamed directly inside it |
| JSON | a plain-text settings format |
| CWD (current folder) | the folder the program was started from; a file name with no folder part is created there |
| regex / pattern | a text-matching rule for file names |

| # | What enters | Who supplies it | Read at (file:line) | What the program takes from it | Feature | Evidence |

| I2 | **The first 8 bytes of each candidate file** | the files themselves | `utils/helpers.py:282-284` | whether the file starts with `PK\x03\x04` or `\xd0\xcf\x11\xe0` (A-R10) | A-F7 | READ; RAN (TRACE T8) |

| I15 | **`requirements.txt`** | the repository | `main.py:84-88` → `utils/dependency_installer.py:33-37` | handed to pip as `-r <file>` (the whole file) | A-F1 | READ; RAN (START) |

> **Important for the author.** The program never reads the *contents* of a client's source file (I3). Client, state, document type and period come only from the file NAME. Everything downstream — the client grouping, the folder each file lands in, which file the report template is told to read — depends on the name following one of the six patterns of A-R1 to A-R6. [GST RULE]

Nothing is sent over the network by the program itself: a whole-program search of the non-test source for `requests`, `urllib`, `socket`, `http.client` found nothing (RAN me again for this section; also DOCS RM40). The only network activity is the optional pip install (O17) and reading/writing the user's own network share. Other channels the audit method asks about are also unused: no clipboard, no drag-and-drop, no command-line options (the arguments are only passed on unchanged when the program restarts itself, `utils/dependency_installer.py:70`), and no environment variable is read directly — the home and TEMP folders come through `Path.home()` and `tempfile.gettempdir()` (I14) (RAN VERIFY-A-S45: a search of the non-test source for `clipboard`, `drag`, `dnd`, `environ`, `getenv`, `argv` found only that restart line).

```

Step 1 edits
  Browse… → dialog → Tk variable → status bar "Source: …" → save settings   gui/handlers/file_handler.py:19-55
  typing → Tk variable only (no save)                                        gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:378-380

What is transformed here:

| Data | Before | After | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| A path | text from a dialog or typed | the same text (not normalised, not checked for being a folder rather than a file) | `gui/handlers/file_handler.py:23, 34, 45, 53` |
| Checklist state | four texts | four (state, message) pairs; a folder row is "✓ <path>" with paths over 46 characters shown as `...` + last 42; a template row is "✓ <file name>" | `gui/utils/setup_validation.py:47-72` |
| Settings | Tk variables | one-line JSON with nine keys plus `recent_folders` | `gui/main_window.py:358-371`; `gui/utils/cache_manager.py:29-46` |

Settings: who writes, who reads (the audit method's "write path vs read path" check):

| Key | Written by (`gui/main_window.py`) | Read by | Agree? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `source_folder` | `:361` | `:376`; `recent_folders` built from it `gui/utils/cache_manager.py:37-40` | yes |
| `itc_template`, `sales_template` | `:362-363` | `:377-378` | yes |
| `target_folder` | `:364` | `:379`; `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:55` (first of four keys); `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:593` | yes (same path `Path.home() / '.gst_organizer_cache.json'` in `gui/utils/cache_manager.py:15`, `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:41`, `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:118`) — HIGH, RAN (me s1) |
| `processing_mode` | `:365` | `:380` (default `'fresh'`) | yes; but the radio never saves (A-R116) |
| `workflow_mode` | `:366` | `:382-383` (default `'organize'`) | yes |
| `include_client_name` | `:367` | `:381` | yes |
| `dark_mode` (A-F28) | `:368` | `:384, 388-389` (switching it on at start-up saves the file again, `:196`) | yes |
| `client_name_max_length` | `:369` | `:386-387` (0 or less → 35) | yes; a non-numeric box makes `IntVar.get()` raise at `:369` and the save is lost (RAN, GUI exp5) |
| `recent_folders` | `gui/utils/cache_manager.py:43` | nobody | write-only (A-R117) |
| (the file as a whole) | rewritten in place on every save (`gui/utils/cache_manager.py:45-46`) | `gui/main_window.py:121, 375-389` | a broken file loads as no settings; a file holding valid JSON that is not an object (e.g. `[1]`, `5`) makes every save fail and stops the main window from being built at start-up (`AttributeError … has no attribute 'get'`) — RAN (me s4), see A-R114 |
| `target_path`, `output_folder`, `last_target` | nobody | `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:55` | reader-only (A-R118) |

#### 4.4.2 Scan and validate (A-F7, A-F8, A-F9, A-F11, A-F12)

```
"Scan Files" / "Re-scan"                                                    gui/handlers/file_handler.py:57, 97-118

How one file becomes data (RAN me s1; RAN CORE exp2 for the full key list):

| Stage | Content | Where |
|---|---|---|
| directory entry | name, cached `stat` (size, dates, type) | `utils/helpers.py:328-339` |
| file record | `name`, `path` (absolute), `size`, `size_formatted`, `modified`, `created`, `extension` (lower case), `is_file`, `is_dir`, `filename`, `parsed`, `pattern`, `type`, `category`, `folder`, `client`, `state`, `metadata` (period text), possibly `warnings`, `error`, plus `full_path`, `relative_path` | `utils/helpers.py:149-163`; `core/file_parser.py:66-75, 89-106, 199-204` |
| client record | `client` and `state` (of the LAST file read for that key), `state_code`, `files` {type → list of file records}, `missing_files`, `extra_files`, `status`, `status_icon`, `total_size`, `file_count`, `completeness` | `core/file_parser.py:35-44, 233-244, 260-286` |
| table row | ✓ box, client, state, "<icon> <status>", number of files, missing types joined by ", " or "None", NUMBER of duplicated types, "☐ No" | `gui/handlers/client_handler.py:31-44` |
| selected key | `"<client>-<state code>"` rebuilt from the table's displayed text | `gui/handlers/client_handler.py:167-171` |

What is lost or changed on the way (each is described in Section 5 and, as a gap, in Section 7):

- Files in sub-folders, `.xlsb`/`.xltx`/`.csv`, files under 1024 bytes, files without an Excel signature and folders named like workbooks are dropped with no count and no message (RAN me s1: `tiny.xlsx`, the `.csv` and `sub/…` never appeared; RAN TRACE T6). See A-R8 to A-R10.
- The period text of every file is kept in `metadata` and never read again (A-R25). [GST RULE]
- The client/state checks of `validate_client_name` are stored in `warnings` and never shown (A-R18).
- The client key is case- and spelling-sensitive (A-R17); the table hands number-looking text back as numbers, so the rebuilt key can differ from the stored key (RAN TRACE T7/T12).
- Duplicates show only as a NUMBER of duplicated types in the table; which file will feed the report is not shown (A-R57).

The status of one report, which decides what the consolidated workbook shows later (A-R73, A-R89):

| Situation | `status.success` | `status.error` / message | Where |

| any error from the window activation onwards (key presses, waits, dialog checks, the `Info!BB2` check, Save, Close — for example `'NoneType' object has no attribute 'Save'` when three "Call was rejected by callee" failures left no workbook open) | False | the error text | `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:258-381` (the inner `try` starts at `:258`; handler `:376-381`) — READ, HIGH |
| any error while starting Excel or opening | False | the error text | `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:388-393` |
| Skip Refresh found a copy | True | None / "Skipped - Using existing refreshed file" | `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:151-155` |

| copy fails (e.g. empty suffix → "are the same file") | False | the error text | `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:198-207` (RAN TRACE T5) |

Where each configured cell actually lands (RAN me s1 with invented marker numbers in every cell; the same result as TRACE T4 Part E and PQ). [GST RULE] for every row — which cell feeds which column is the author's decision; the table only records what the code does. ⚠ sheet names and cells follow the template version.

| Output sheet | Column | Configured source | Marker value in the source | Value written | Same as configured? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GSTR-3B Difference | Diff IGST / Diff CGST / Diff SGST | `Diff GSTR-3B`!J15 / K15 / L15 | 101 / 102 / 103 | 101 / 102 / 103 | yes |
| GSTR-1 Difference | Diff Taxable Value / Diff IGST / Diff CGST / Diff SGST | `Diff - GSTR-1`!N1 / O1 / P1 / Q1 | 801 / 802 / 0 / empty | 801 / 802 / 0 (unformatted) / "Missing" | yes |
| Turnover GSTR-1 | Taxable Value | `Diff - GSTR-1`!I1 | 800 | 800 | yes |
| Reco Diff | IGST Diff / CGST Diff / SGST Diff | `Reco`!C37 / D37 / E37 | 201 / 202 / 203 | 201 / 202 / 203 | yes |
| Table 6J Match | Table 6J Match | `T6 of R9`!K29 | Yes | Yes | yes |
| Table 8D Limits | Table 8D Limits | `T8 of R9`!G6 | 401.5 | 401.5 | yes |
| IMS Reco | IGST / CGST / SGST | `IMS_Reco`!AA1 / AD1 / AG1 | 501 / 502 / 503 | **101 / 102 / 103** | **no** |
| Extra In Purchase | IGST / CGST / SGST | `ExtraInPurchase`!O1 / P1 / Q1 | 601 / 602 / 603 | **101 / 102 / 103** | **no** |
| Extra In GSTR2B | IGST / CGST / SGST | `ExtraInGSTR2B`!S1 / T1 / U1 | 701 / 702 / 703 | **101 / 102 / 103** | **no** |

> **Technical mismatch, not a GST judgement.** In the last three sheets the column titles are the single words `IGST`, `CGST`, `SGST`. The lookup at `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:142-145` takes the first stored key that merely *contains* the title, and the first such key is `GSTR-3B_Difference_Diff_IGST` (the GSTR-3B Difference sheet is first in the configuration). So these nine columns show `Diff GSTR-3B`!J15/K15/L15 whenever those cells hold a value (0 included); only when they came back as nothing (`None`: an empty cell, an unreadable cell or a missing `Diff GSTR-3B` sheet) does the exact key give the configured cell (RAN TRACE T4: a client without the `Diff GSTR-3B` sheet showed the configured values). If J15/K15/L15 hold an empty TEXT (for example a formula that returns ""), the column shows "Missing" instead of the configured cell (RAN VERIFY-A-S45 `v_consolidator.py`: J15 `None` → the AA1 marker, J15 `''` → `Missing`, J15 `0` → `0`, J15 `7` → `7`). Which cell is meant for each column is the author's call (see A-R80 to A-R82, A-R88 in Section 5).

#### 4.4.7 Side flows

| Flow | Path through the code | Output |

### 4.6 Worked example (RAN me, `s1_end_to_end.py`)

Inputs (all invented): 11 workbooks of at least 1024 bytes in the source folder plus `tiny.xlsx` (104 bytes), a `.csv` and a GSTR-3B workbook in a sub-folder `sub/`; two templates each with a `Links` sheet (placeholders in B10/B11), an `Info` sheet (`BB2` = `<Client A>`) and every sheet Step 4 reads, filled with marker numbers.

| Step | What came out |
|---|---|
| Scan | 11 files listed (the tiny file, the `.csv` and the sub-folder file absent, no message); 2 clients: `Alpha Test Traders-MH` — "Has duplicates", 100.0 %, extras `Multiple IMS Reco (2 files)` (two GSTR-3B files are not duplicates); `Beta Demo Industries Private Limited-GJ` — "Missing 4 files", 33.3 %; 1 variation `Random notes.xlsx` → "Check file naming convention"; statistics: 11 files, 10 parsed, parsing rate 90.9 %, 0 complete clients ("Clients with Missing Files: 2", the duplicate-only client included) |
| Organise | `Annual Statement-211026 1030/` with `Alpha Test Traders-MH/` and `Beta Demo Industries Pvt Ltd-GJ/`, each `Version-211026 1030/` with `GSTR3B/`, `ITC/`, `Sales/` (empty ones too); the Annual Report in the Version folder itself; 10 copies with cleaned names (`(1)_GSTR3B-…`, `…31032025_(1).xlsx`, `FY_2024-25`) |
| Reports | `ITC_Report_Alpha_Test_Traders_MH_211026_1030.xlsx` etc.; Links as in 4.4.4 (B11 = the `_(1)` IMS copy; Beta's ITC report kept the template placeholders in B10/B11); log "✅ Success" for all four although the openpyxl fallback ran ("Not on Windows - Power Query may not be preserved!") |
| Text reports | `_Organization_Report_Alpha Test Traders_Maharashtra.txt`, `_Organization_Report_Beta Demo Industries Private Limited_Gujarat.txt` (raw names) |
| Summary | `GST_Processing_Summary_211026_1030.xlsx`, contents as in 4.4.4 |
| Step 4 scan | Level 1 = `Annual Statement-211026 1030`; clients listed in directory order (`Beta…` before `Alpha…` on this disk), each with ITC and Sales |
| Step 4 naming | refreshed copies `…_211026_1030_Refreshed_211026_0640.xlsx` (the time of the run) made with `shutil.copy2`; refresh and extraction stubbed |
| Consolidated | `PQ_Extraction_Report_20261021_0640.xlsx`, Summary + 9 sheets, values as in the 4.4.6 table (nine cross-wired cells) |
| Settings | `{"source_folder": …, "target_folder": …, "client_name_max_length": 35, "recent_folders": [ … ]}` on one line; the standalone extractor read `target_folder` back. (The script called `CacheManager.save_cache` directly with only these three keys; the real window always writes the nine keys of `gui/main_window.py:360-370` plus `recent_folders`, see 4.4.1.) |

### 4.7 Points where data is lost, changed or cross-wired (technical summary)

These are recorded here because they are properties of the data flow; Section 7 will number them as gaps. GST rules are only described.

1. **Silent input loss** — sub-folders, `.xlsb`, `.xltx`, `.csv`, files under 1024 bytes, files without a ZIP/OLE signature and any listing error vanish without a count (`utils/helpers.py:327-365`; A-R8 to A-R10). RAN (me s1).
2. **Period information discarded** — every period, month, date and year part of a file name is stored and never read; files of different periods of one client are grouped together (`core/file_parser.py:97-106`; A-R25). [GST RULE]
3. **First file wins, silently** — when a client has several files of one type, only the first in sorted full-path order is named in the Links sheet (`utils/helpers.py:361`; `core/excel_handler.py:628-699`; A-R57). RAN (me s1: the `(1)` IMS copy). [GST RULE]
4. **Template content survives where a client lacks a file** — empty values are not written, so the template's existing cell content stays (`core/excel_handler.py:192, 300`; A-R56). RAN (me s1).
5. **Three forms of the client name** (4.4.3) — two clients can share one Level-2 folder (abbreviation variants, first characters equal, letter case on Windows); Step 4 then sees one client folder (A-R35; RAN TRACE T6 §7, T9). Whether their REPORTS stay apart depends on the case: clients whose long names agree only in the first characters keep separate report names (the report name uses the full cleaned name, RAN TRACE T9), but abbreviation variants (`<Client A> Private Limited` / `<Client A> Pvt Ltd`) clean to the SAME report name, so the second client's ITC and Sales reports overwrite the first's in the shared Version folder while the log counts four reports created (RAN VERIFY-A-S45 `v_abbrev_merge.py`: one `ITC_Report_…` and one `Sales_Report_…` on disk, "Reports created: 4"); case-only variants do the same on Windows' case-insensitive disks (A-R59, READ, MEDIUM). [Corrected during verification — originally stated that their reports keep separate names in every case.]
6. **Power Query dropped off Windows** — the openpyxl fallback saves the report without its queries and the run still reports success (`core/excel_handler.py:96-102, 262-310`; A-R96). RAN (XL E1, CORE exp5).
7. **A failed refresh still leaves a "refreshed" copy** dated like the original, later shown as the last refresh and reused by Skip Refresh as a success (`power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:164, 137-155`; A-R69). RAN (TRACE T10).
8. **Figures hidden after a failed refresh** — values read from a report whose status is not success are replaced by "Missing" (`power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:128-129`; A-R89). RAN (TRACE T4).
9. **Nine cross-wired columns** in the consolidated workbook (4.4.6; A-R88). RAN (me s1).
10. **Output folder of Step 4 decided by the LAST scan**, also mid-run (`power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:632, 945`; A-R86). RAN (PQ).
11. **Same-minute names overwrite** — Level 1, Version, reports, summary, backups, refreshed copies and the consolidated workbook all have minute resolution (A-R33, A-R46, A-R67, A-R86). RAN (TRACE T6 runs 2-4, T8 §4).
12. **Client data in log files** in whatever folder the program was started from (`main.py:24`; A-R120, A-R122).

## SECTION 5: BUSINESS RULES AND DOMAIN LOGIC

This section documents every rule of the registry (`/root/merge-scratch/audit/A_registry.md`, A-R1 to A-R157), grouped in the registry's 16 categories and in ID order, followed by every other hard-coded value, a comparison of rules that exist in several places, the older documents that disagree with the code, and questions for the author. It is the section to rebuild from.

### 5.0 How to read this section

- **Each rule** has: its ID and name; tags; *What the code does* (with the exact values); *Where* (every `file:line` that implements or repeats it, at snapshot `bf2f1dd`; an old version is written `path:line@commit`); *Evidence* (READ = read in the code; RAN = executed on made-up inputs in a scratch copy; "RAN (me)" with `s1` … `s4` = this section's scripts `/root/merge-scratch/run/A-S45/scripts/s1_end_to_end.py`, `s2_rule_values.py`, `s3_more_values.py`, `s4_cache_shapes.py`, outputs in `/root/merge-scratch/run/A-S45/outputs/`; "RAN VERIFY-A-S45" = the independent verification pass described in 4.0 (scripts in `/root/merge-scratch/run/VERIFY-A-S45/scripts/`); other RAN labels name the working note — CORE, PQ, GUI, START, DCP, XL, EXT, TRACE, DOCS as in the registry); *History* where a commit matters; *Older documents* where they disagree.
- **Tags.**
  - **[GST RULE]** — the code decides a tax figure, which return / table / sheet / cell a figure or file belongs to, a rate, limit or threshold, rounding, the financial year or tax period, GSTIN / PAN / state-code logic, ITC eligibility or reversal, or a due date. For these rules this section says exactly what the code does and nothing about whether it is correct. Several tags are conservative ("when in doubt").
  - **⚠** — a value that can change over time (return or table names, template cell positions, state lists, file-naming conventions, Excel behaviour, package versions). The author should check these.
  - **DEAD** — defined but never used by the program (it may still be used by tests).
  - "not a GST rule" — purely technical or presentational.
- **Commits.** The 12 commits shared by A and B (`edac6a3` … `47a1492`) are marked "shared - before the split on 30-10-2025"; A's later commits "A only (after the split)".
- **Confidence.** HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW on every inference.
- Terms explained in Section 4.0 are not repeated. New terms here: **stem** (a file name without its extension), **8.3 short path** (an old DOS-style short alias of a long path, e.g. `PROGRA~1`), **UNC path** (a network path written `\\server\share\…`), **DispatchEx** (a way to start a separate, private Excel rather than borrowing a running one), **SendKeys** (pressing keyboard keys by program into whatever window has the focus), **find_spec** (a Python check that a module exists without loading it), **daemon thread** (a worker that is killed instantly when the program exits), **Treeview** (Tk's table widget — the Step 2 client list), **IntVar** (a Tk variable holding a whole number — the length box), **TclError** (the error Tk raises, e.g. when a number box holds text), **ttk style** (a named look shared by all widgets of one kind, e.g. every tab), **GetActiveObject / Dispatch** (two ways to get an Excel through COM: grab the one already running / start one, which may still attach to a running one), **BM_CLICK / WM_CLOSE** (Windows messages that press a button / ask a window to close), **Xvfb** (an invisible screen used to run window code on this Linux machine), **shallow copy** (a copy of a record whose inner parts are still shared with the original).

After the 16 categories come: 5.18 UNREGISTERED rules, 5.19 every other hard-coded value, 5.20 rules that exist in several places with different values, 5.21 older documents that disagree, 5.22 the [GST RULE] and ⚠ register, 5.23 questions for the author.

### 5.2 Category 1 — Input-file recognition (A-R1 to A-R16)

All six name patterns live in one dictionary, `FILE_PATTERNS` (`utils/constants.py:13-62`), and are tried in its order by `core/file_parser.py:82-128`. The full table first; each pattern then has its own entry.

| Order | Key | Regex (verbatim) | Type given | `category` / `folder` (never read, A-R16) | Parts kept | Description shown on Step 1 | Line |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `GSTR-2B-Reco` | `^GSTR-2B-Reco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(.+)\.xlsx?$` | `GSTR-2B Reco` | `ITC` / `itc` | client, state, period | `GSTR-2B-Reco-ClientName-State-Period.xlsx` | `utils/constants.py:15` |
| 2 | `ImsReco` | `^ImsReco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(\d{8})(?:\s*\(\d+\))?\.xlsx?$` | `IMS Reco` | `ITC` / `itc` | client, state, date | `ImsReco-ClientName-State-DDMMYYYY.xlsx` | `:23` |
| 3 | `GSTR3B` | `^(?:\(\d+\)\s*)?GSTR3B-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-([^-]+)\.xlsx?$` | `GSTR-3B Export` | `GSTR3B` / `gstr3b` | client, state, month | `GSTR3B-ClientName-State-Month.xlsx` | `:31` |
| 4 | `Sales` | `^Sales-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-([^-]+)\.xlsx?$` | `Sales` | `Sales` / `sales` | client, state, start_month, end_month | `Sales-ClientName-State-StartMonth-EndMonth.xlsx` | `:39` |
| 5 | `SalesReco` | `^SalesReco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(.+)\.xlsx?$` | `Sales Reco` | `Sales` / `sales` | client, state, period | `SalesReco-ClientName-State-Period.xlsx` | `:47` |
| 6 | `AnnualReport` | `^AnnualReport-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(.+)\.xlsx?$` | `Annual Report` | `Annual` / `root` | client, state, year | `AnnualReport-ClientName-State-Year.xlsx` | `:55` |

Every pattern is compiled with `re.IGNORECASE` (any letter case) on the same line. In plain words: `[^-]+` = "one or more characters that are not a hyphen"; `(.+)` = "everything that is left, hyphens included"; `\d{8}` = "exactly eight digits"; `\.xlsx?$` = "ends in `.xls` or `.xlsx`".

#### A-R1 — GSTR-2B Reco file name · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** A name matching `^GSTR-2B-Reco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(.+)\.xlsx?$` in any letter case is type `GSTR-2B Reco`. Part 1 = client, part 2 = state, part 3 = period (the period may contain hyphens, so `Apr24-Mar25` is one period). Shown to the user as `GSTR-2B-Reco-ClientName-State-Period.xlsx`.
- **Where.** Regex `utils/constants.py:15`; type `:16`; description `:19`; matched `core/file_parser.py:82-128`; description shown on the Step 1 card `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:494-503`; the same description re-typed as the suggestion text `core/file_parser.py:294`.
- **Evidence.** READ. RAN (TRACE T1 rows 1-10): `…-Apr24-Mar25.xlsx` → period `Apr24-Mar25`; `.xls` accepted; `.xlsm` rejected (variation); `GSTR2B-Reco-…` (no hyphen) rejected; a hyphenated client `Alpha-Test-Traders` is accepted as client `Alpha`, state `Test`, period `Traders-Maharashtra-Q1` with no warning.
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); unchanged.
- **⚠** The return name and the portal's file-naming convention can change.

#### A-R2 — IMS Reco file name · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** `^ImsReco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(\d{8})(?:\s*\(\d+\))?\.xlsx?$` (any case) → type `IMS Reco`; client, state, and a date of exactly 8 digits, never checked to be a real date (`99999999` and `20250331` are accepted). One optional duplicate marker `(N)` or ` (N)` after the date is allowed and is not kept in the date. Shown as `ImsReco-ClientName-State-DDMMYYYY.xlsx`.
- **Where.** `utils/constants.py:23` (regex), `:24` (type), `:27` (description); `core/file_parser.py:82-128`; suggestion text `core/file_parser.py:296`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T1 rows 11-18: 6-digit date and `Mar25` rejected; `IMS Reco-…` with a space rejected; `…31032025 (1).xlsx` and `…31032025(2).xlsx` accepted with date `31032025`); RAN (EXT t1: `… (1) (2).xlsx` rejected — only one marker).
- **History.** The `(N)` part was added in `5c8ce95` (A only (after the split)); at the split the regex was `^ImsReco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(\d{8})\.xlsx?$` (`utils/constants.py:23@47a1492`); the rest since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **⚠** Date format and return name.

#### A-R3 — GSTR-3B Export file name · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** `^(?:\(\d+\)\s*)?GSTR3B-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-([^-]+)\.xlsx?$` (any case) → type `GSTR-3B Export`; client, state, month; no part may contain a hyphen. An optional LEADING `(N)` or `(N) ` is accepted (only this pattern accepts a leading marker). A trailing ` (1)` is not a separate marker: it becomes part of the month (`Apr (1)`). `GSTR-3B-…` (hyphenated keyword) is not recognised. Shown as `GSTR3B-ClientName-State-Month.xlsx`.
- **Where.** `utils/constants.py:31` (regex), `:32`, `:35`; `core/file_parser.py:82-128`; suggestion `core/file_parser.py:298`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T1 rows 19-31; me s1: `(1) GSTR3B-Alpha Test Traders-Maharashtra-May.xlsx` recognised).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Older documents.** `PROJECT_AUDIT_ANNUALSTATEMENT.md:471` places the `(1)` prefix at `constants.py:32`; it is at `:31` (DOCS R4).
- **⚠** Return name, file naming.

#### A-R4 — Sales file name · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** `^Sales-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-([^-]+)\.xlsx?$` (any case) → type `Sales`; exactly four parts: client, state, start_month, end_month. One or three period parts → not recognised. `Sales-Reco-<client>-<state>-Q1.xlsx` is read as a SALES file of client `Reco` (state = the real client, start = the real state). Shown as `Sales-ClientName-State-StartMonth-EndMonth.xlsx`.
- **Where.** `utils/constants.py:39`, `:40`, `:43`; `core/file_parser.py:82-128`; suggestion `core/file_parser.py:302`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T1 rows 32-37; EXT t1; registry check1.py).

- **What the code does.** `^SalesReco-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(.+)\.xlsx?$` (any case) → type `Sales Reco`; client, state, period (may contain hyphens). `Sales Reco-…` (with a space) is not recognised. Shown as `SalesReco-ClientName-State-Period.xlsx`.

- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).

#### A-R6 — Annual Report file name · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** `^AnnualReport-([^-]+)-([^-]+)-(.+)\.xlsx?$` (any case) → type `Annual Report`; client, state, year (may contain hyphens). `Annual Report-…` (space) is not recognised; a name without a year part is not recognised. Shown as `AnnualReport-ClientName-State-Year.xlsx`.
- **Where.** `utils/constants.py:55`, `:56`, `:59`; `core/file_parser.py:82-128`; suggestion `core/file_parser.py:304`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T1 rows 40-44: `…-2024-25 (1).xlsx` → year `2024-25 (1)`; `…-2024_Refreshed_211026_1100.xlsx` accepted as an Annual Report).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **⚠** Return/report name, file naming.

#### A-R7 — How a name is matched · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.**
  1. The six patterns are tried in the order of A-R1 to A-R6; the first match wins (`break`).
  2. All ignore letter case; all accept only `.xlsx` or `.xls` (so `.xlsm` is listed by the scan, A-R8, but can never match).
  3. Each captured part is trimmed of surrounding spaces; an empty part is skipped (so an all-space client becomes `''`).
  4. Because client and state are `[^-]+`, a hyphen inside either shifts the parts: A-R1, A-R5 and A-R6 then accept the file with client and state taken from the first hyphen-separated pieces (e.g. `Alpha-Test-Traders` gives client `Alpha`, state `Test`, RAN VERIFY-A-S45 `v_patterns.py`), not the text the person meant; A-R2, A-R3 and A-R4 reject it (technical splitting behaviour; which naming the author expects is the author's call).
  5. The six fixed beginnings (`GSTR-2B-Reco-`, `ImsReco-`, optional `(N)` + `GSTR3B-`, `Sales-`, `SalesReco-`, `AnnualReport-`) cannot both match one name, so the order does not change any result today.
  6. A match fills `type`, `category`, `folder`, `pattern`, `client`, `state`, `metadata` (the remaining parts); no match → `error = 'No matching pattern found'` and a WARNING log line `Could not parse filename: <name>` (client file names reach the log).
- **Where.** `core/file_parser.py:82-128` (first match, `break` at `:128`), `:98-106` (trim at `:102, :104, :106`; skip empty at `:100`), `:130-132` (no match).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T1: at most one pattern matched each of 60 names).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).

#### A-R8 — Which files are looked at
- **What the code does.** Only the top level of the source folder is listed (one `os.scandir`, no sub-folders). A name is kept if, lower-cased, it ends in `.xlsx`, `.xls` or `.xlsm`. It must then be a regular file (a folder named `x.xlsx` is skipped) and have a suffix in a second list `.xlsx`, `.xls`, `.xlsm`, `.xltx`, `.xltm` (which adds nothing after the first filter). Anything else (`.xlsb`, `.xltx`, `.csv`, files in sub-folders) is dropped with no message, no count and no variation entry. If an entry cannot be read between listing and checking it is skipped (`OSError`).
- **Exact values.** First filter `('.xlsx', '.xls', '.xlsm')`; second filter `{'.xlsx', '.xls', '.xlsm', '.xltx', '.xltm'}`.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:327` (first list), `:328` (one listing), `:330` (test), `:332-338` (vanished entry), `:296` (regular file), `:299` (second list). Used by `core/file_parser.py:158`. The test-only twins `utils/helpers.py:240-260, 368-373` repeat the same checks.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s1: the `.csv` and the sub-folder file never appeared; TRACE T6).
- **History.** One listing since `a603c02` / `56aaad9` (A only (after the split)); at the split three globs (`utils/helpers.py:249-257@47a1492`).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R9 — Minimum file size
- **What the code does.** A candidate must be at least **1024 bytes**; smaller files (including Excel's own `~$` lock files of about 165 bytes) are dropped silently.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:302-303`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T8: 1023 bytes rejected, 1024 accepted; me s1: a 104-byte file dropped).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R10 — Excel signature
- **What the code does.** The first 8 bytes are read; the file is kept only if they start with `PK\x03\x04` (ZIP-based `.xlsx/.xlsm/.xltx/.xltm`) or `\xd0\xcf\x11\xe0` (old `.xls`). A wrong signature is dropped with no log line; an unreadable file is dropped with an ERROR log line. Any ZIP file renamed `.xlsx` passes.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:264` (`_XLSX_SIG`), `:265` (`_XLS_SIG`), `:275-287` (`_has_excel_signature`, read `:282-283`, test `:284`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T8: a plain `.zip` renamed `.xlsx` accepted).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R11 — Unrecognised files become "variations"
- **What the code does.** A file that passed A-R8 to A-R10 but matches no pattern becomes a variation record `{filename, path, issue: 'No matching pattern found', size (bytes), pattern_expected (A-R12)}`. A file that matched but has an empty client or state becomes a variation with issue `'Could not parse filename'`, while the scan statistics still count it as parsed. Variations are never copied; Step 2 shows only their number; the list appears as the "Variations" sheet of the Step 3 summary workbook (A-R61).
- **Where.** `core/file_parser.py:130-132` (issue text), `:209-220` (variation record), counted `:311` (parsed) and `:329` (variations); shown `gui/main_window.py:320-321`; written `core/excel_handler.py:554-585`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T11: 3 files → parsed 2, unparsed 1, variations 2; me s1: `Random notes.xlsx`).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R12 — Suggested pattern for an unrecognised name · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** Checked on the lower-cased name, first hit wins:

| Test | Suggestion |
|---|---|
| contains `gstr` and `2b` | `GSTR-2B-Reco-ClientName-State-Period.xlsx` |
| else contains `ims` | `ImsReco-ClientName-State-DDMMYYYY.xlsx` |
| else contains `gstr` and `3b` | `GSTR3B-ClientName-State-Month.xlsx` |
| else contains `sales` and `reco` | `SalesReco-ClientName-State-Period.xlsx` |
| else contains `sales` | `Sales-ClientName-State-StartMonth-EndMonth.xlsx` |
| else contains `annual` | `AnnualReport-ClientName-State-Year.xlsx` |
| none | `Check file naming convention` |

  Plain substring tests: a name containing `claims` gets the IMS text. Only one suggestion is ever produced, although the code joins suggestions with `" or "`.
- **Where.** `core/file_parser.py:288-306`; the six texts duplicate `utils/constants.py:19, 27, 35, 43, 51, 59`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2: `claims register.xlsx` → IMS suggestion; `notes.xlsx` → "Check file naming convention").
- **⚠** Return names.

#### A-R13 — `EXCEL_EXTENSIONS = {'.xlsx', '.xls', '.xlsm'}` · DEAD
- **What the code does.** Defined and re-exported; never used — the scan types its own lists (A-R8).

- **What the code does.** GSTR-2B Reco `ITC`/`itc`; IMS Reco `ITC`/`itc`; GSTR-3B `GSTR3B`/`gstr3b`; Sales `Sales`/`sales`; Sales Reco `Sales`/`sales`; Annual Report `Annual`/`root`. Copied into every parse result and never read afterwards. The Annual Report label `root` differs from where the organiser really puts the file (the Version folder, A-R41).

### 5.3 Category 2 — Client identity and name cleaning (A-R17 to A-R20)

#### A-R17 — Client key · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** Recognised files are grouped under the key `"<client text exactly as written in the file name>-<state code>"` (state code from A-R21 / A-R22). The key is case- and space-sensitive, so `<Client A> Private Limited`, `<Client A> Pvt Ltd` and a lower-case spelling are three clients. Two state spellings that give the same code merge into one client, and the client's stored `client` and `state` are those of the LAST file read for that key (e.g. `Orissa` and `Odisha` merge, shown as whichever came last). The GUI rebuilds the key from the table's displayed text; Tk returns number-looking text as numbers (`0042` → 42), giving keys that do not exist.
- **Where.** Built `core/file_parser.py:229-236`; rebuilt `gui/handlers/client_handler.py:169-170, 181, 218`; `core/file_organizer.py:122`; looked up `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:56, 101, 232`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6/T8: spelling variants → separate clients; T7/T12: `0042` → `42-MH`, a state `27` crashes `get_state_code`).
- **History.** Key by state code since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); the GUI's per-client-flag key was by state NAME until `33d0baf` (A only (after the split)).

#### A-R18 — Client and state text checks · DEAD (output never read)
- **What the code does.** `validate_client_name` returns (False, message) for text that is empty or blank (`"Client name cannot be empty"`), shorter than **2** (`"Client name too short"`), longer than **100** (`"Client name too long"`) or containing any of `< > : " / \ | ? *` (`"Client name contains invalid characters"`); otherwise (True, ""). It is applied to the client AND to the state (the state message reuses the client wording: `"State name issue: Client name too short"`). The result goes into the parse result's `warnings`, which nothing reads — so it changes nothing.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:428-449` (2 at `:438`, 100 at `:441`, characters `:445`); applied `core/file_parser.py:108-119`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2: all five branches; TRACE T1 rows 30-31, 51).

#### A-R19 — Business abbreviations
- **What the code does.** Whole words `Private` → `Pvt` and `Limited` → `Ltd`, any letter case; words that merely contain them (`Privateer`, `Unlimited`) are untouched. There are TWO routines:
  - in file names and the Level-4 suffix (`sanitize_filename`) runs of spaces/underscores are first turned into single spaces, so `X_Private_Limited` is abbreviated (`X_Pvt_Ltd`);
  - in the Level-2 folder name (`create_client_state_key`) this step is missing, so `X_Private_Limited` keeps the full words.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:91-93` (file names), `:624-625` (Level-2 key).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2: `Zeta_Private_Limited` → `Zeta_Pvt_Ltd` in file names, `Zeta_Private_Limited-DL` as the folder; `Privateer Unlimited` unchanged).
- **History.** The key version has used whole-word, case-insensitive matching since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025) (`utils/helpers.py:517-518@47a1492`). The file-name version was a case-sensitive substring replace (`base_name.replace('Private', 'Pvt')`, `utils/helpers.py:78-79@47a1492`, which turned `Privateer` into `Pvter`) until `33d0baf`; the underscore step was added in `4219ffb` (both A only (after the split)).
- **Older documents.** `PROJECT_AUDIT_ANNUALSTATEMENT.md:500-507` describes the old case-sensitive replace (DOCS R-SAN); `tests/README.md:27-31` names the `Privateer` quirk, which the same day's change removed (DOCS T9).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R20 — Name cleaning (`sanitize_filename`)
- **What the code does**, in this order:
  1. empty text → `unnamed`;
  2. the "extension" = the text after the LAST dot (`Path.suffix`), put back unchanged at the end; the rest is the stem;
  3. abbreviations as A-R19;
  4. control characters (codes 0-31 and 127-159) → `_`;
  5. each of `` < > : " / \ | ? * [ ] { } + = ! @ # $ % ^ , ; ' " ` ~ `` (one character each; the backtick is one of them) → `_` (hyphen, dot, `&`, `(`, `)` are kept; `"` is listed twice);
  6. runs of spaces/underscores → one `_`;
  7. `_`, `.` and spaces stripped at both ends; empty → `unnamed`;
  8. the stem cut to `max_length − len(extension) − 10` characters (default `max_length` 200 → 185 characters + `.xlsx` = 190 in total).
  Because the extension is the text after the last dot, client names with a dot are only partly cleaned (`Omega Co. Private Limited` → `Omega_Co. Private Limited`), and a trailing dot behaves differently on Python 3.12/3.13 and 3.14 (RAN CORE exp3b).
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:68-126` (default 200 `:68`, `unnamed` `:72-73, 115-116`, stem/suffix `:76-77`, characters `:96`, control `:99-102`, collapse `:109`, strip `:112`, length `:119-120`, extension back `:123-124`). Used for copied file names `core/file_organizer.py:271`, the Level-4 client suffix `core/file_organizer.py:90`, report names `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:292`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2: `Alpha's #1, Shop!.xlsx` → `Alpha_s_1_Shop.xlsx`; `___` → `unnamed`; a 250-character name → 190 characters; `Omega Co. Private Limited` → `Omega_Co. Private Limited`).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R22 — Code for a state name not in the table · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** Empty text or `None` is returned unchanged. Otherwise, if the trimmed, lower-cased text is not in the table:
  - two or more words → the first letters of the FIRST TWO words, upper-cased (`Unknown Place` → `UP`, the same code as Uttar Pradesh; `Daman and Diu` → `DA`; `Jammu & Kashmir` → `J&`; `NCT of Delhi` → `NO`);

  - a WARNING `Unknown state '<text>', using fallback: <code>` is logged.
  A number (which Tk hands back for a number-looking table cell, A-R17) crashes at `.strip()`.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:592-618` (empty `:594-595`, two words `:609-613`, one word `:614-618`). The test `tests/test_helpers.py:96-97` pins `Unknown Place` → `UP`.

### 5.5 Category 4 — Financial year and tax period (A-R25)

#### A-R25 — Period parts are captured but never used; no financial-year or tax-period logic · [GST RULE] · ⚠ · DEAD (metadata never read)
- **What the code does.** The parse result keeps, as plain text in `metadata`: `period` (GSTR-2B Reco, Sales Reco), `date` (IMS Reco, 8 digits), `month` (GSTR-3B), `start_month` / `end_month` (Sales), `year` (Annual Report). Nothing reads `metadata`. So:
  - files of different periods or years for one client are grouped together and never ordered or rejected (`13`, `31022025`, `FY`, `x`, `Mar-Apr` are all accepted);
  - when several periods of one type exist, the one named in the report is decided by the alphabetical order of the full paths (A-R57), e.g. `…-2023-24` before `…-2024-25`, `01042025` before `31032025` (day first);
  - the run time stamps in folder names (A-R33) are clock times, not tax periods.
- **Where.** Stored `core/file_parser.py:97-106` (`:106`); the only other appearance is `core/file_parser.py:74` (initial empty dict) and `tests/test_file_patterns.py:67`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T3 (a), (b)).
- **⚠** Financial-year and filing-period conventions.

- **What the code does.** Statistics: total files (= files listed and parsed or not), parsed, unparsed, clients, complete clients, incomplete clients (every client not `Complete`, so duplicate-only clients appear under "Clients with Missing Files"), variations, total size (1024-based units, 1 decimal: `B`, `KB`, `MB`, `GB`, `TB`; negative → `Unknown`), files per type, parsing rate % (0 when no files), completion rate % (0 when no clients). The Step 2 text uses fixed headings: `📊 SCAN RESULTS SUMMARY:`, `📁 FILES ANALYSIS:` (Total Excel Files Found, ✅ Successfully Parsed, ❌ Unparsed/Variations, 📊 Parsing Success Rate x.x%), `👥 CLIENTS ANALYSIS:` (Total Clients Identified, ✅ Clients with All Files, ⚠️ Clients with Missing Files, 📈 Completeness Rate x.x%), `📋 FILE TYPE DISTRIBUTION:` (• <type>: N files), `🔍 FILE VARIATIONS: N files` (only if any), `💾 TOTAL DATA SIZE: …`.

### 5.7 Category 6 — Folder layout and naming (A-R32 to A-R42)

The tree Step 3 builds (RAN me s1; RAN TRACE T6; names invented):

```
<target folder>                                  created if missing        core/file_organizer.py:65-69
└── Annual Statement-<DDMMYY HHMM>               Level 1 (A-R32)           utils/constants.py:82
    ├── GST_Processing_Summary_<DDMMYY_HHMM>.xlsx                          gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:462-464
    ├── PQ_Extraction_Report_<YYYYMMDD_HHMM>.xlsx (later, Step 4)          power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:175-177
    └── <client>-<STATE CODE>                    Level 2 (A-R35)           utils/helpers.py:620-644
        └── Version-<DDMMYY HHMM>                Level 3 (A-R38)           utils/constants.py:84
            ├── GSTR3B[ (<cleaned client>)]      Level 4 (A-R39, A-R40)    utils/constants.py:92
            ├── ITC[ (<cleaned client>)]                                   utils/constants.py:93
            ├── Sales[ (<cleaned client>)]                                 utils/constants.py:94
            ├── <Annual Report copies>           (A-R41)                   core/file_organizer.py:311
            ├── ITC_Report_… / Sales_Report_…    (A-R59)                   gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:297-298, 355-356
            ├── <stem>_Refreshed_<DDMMYY_HHMM>.xlsx (later, Step 4, A-R67)
            └── _Organization_Report_<client>_<state>.txt (A-R62)          core/file_organizer.py:330
```

#### A-R32 — Level-1 (run) folder name
- **What the code does.** The run folder is named `Annual Statement-{timestamp}`, with the run time stamp of A-R33 (for example `Annual Statement-211026 1030`, 28 characters). In Fresh mode it is created with "exist is fine" (`mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`), so a second Fresh run started in the SAME minute silently reuses the same folder (and the same Version folders, A-R38). In Re-run / Resume mode the same name is used only when no earlier `Annual Statement-*` folder exists (A-R43).
- **Where.** Template `utils/constants.py:82`; used `core/file_organizer.py:165-173` (Fresh) and `:188-196` (Re-run/Resume with none found); time stamp taken once per run at `core/file_organizer.py:43` → `utils/helpers.py:25-27`; folder creation `utils/helpers.py:41-49`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s1: `Annual Statement-211026 1030`; TRACE T6 run 2: a same-minute Fresh run wrote into the same folder).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule** (a clock time, not a tax period).

#### A-R33 — Run time stamps (clock time, minute resolution)
- **What the code does.** Every name the program builds carries the LOCAL clock time at minute resolution, in these formats:

| Used for | Format (Python `strftime`) | Example (21-10-2026 10:30) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Level-1 and Version folder names | `%d%m%y %H%M` (day-month-year, space, hour-minute) | `211026 1030` | `utils/helpers.py:25-27`; taken `core/file_organizer.py:43` |
| ITC/Sales report and summary file names | the same, taken AGAIN at `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:186`, with the space replaced by `_` | `211026_1030` | `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:186, 294, 462` |
| Backup copies | `%d%m%y_%H%M`, taken at the moment of the backup | `211026_1031` | `utils/helpers.py:33-35, 224` |
| Refreshed copies (Step 4) | `%d%m%y_%H%M`, taken per report | `211026_1100` | `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:128` |
| Consolidated workbook | `%Y%m%d_%H%M` (year first) | `20261021_1100` | `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:175` |
| Exported client list (suggested name) | `%Y%m%d_%H%M%S` | `20261021_103015` | `gui/handlers/client_handler.py:236` |
| Text inside reports ("Generated: …") | `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S` | `2026-10-21 10:30:15` | `core/file_organizer.py:336`; `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:231`; `gui/handlers/client_handler.py:243` |
| "Last Refresh Status" column | `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M` of the file's date modified | `2026-10-21 11:00` | `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:80` |
| Log lines on screen; Errors sheet "Time" | `%H:%M:%S` | `10:30:15` | `gui/utils/ui_helpers.py:19`; `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:1024`; `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:447` |
| Summary sheet "Processing Date" | the folder stamp TEXT `%d%m%y %H%M` (not a date value) | `211026 1030` | `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:210` → `core/excel_handler.py:443` |

  Two separate clock reads name one run (`core/file_organizer.py:43` for folders, `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:186` for report and summary names); if a minute boundary falls between them, the report names carry a different minute from their Version folder (READ; LOW likelihood — the two lines run microseconds apart). Day-first text does not sort by date (`Version-311225 2359` sorts after `Version-020726 0900`, RAN TRACE T3c), which is why "newest" is decided by date modified (A-R42, A-R43). `get_date_only()` (`%d%m%y`) is dead (A-R34).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s3: `211026 1344` / `211026_1344`; TRACE T3 (c)).
- **Not a GST rule.** These are run times, not financial years or tax periods (A-R25).

#### A-R34 — `get_date_only()` · DEAD
- **What the code does.** Returns `datetime.now().strftime("%d%m%y")`; never called, only re-exported.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:29-31`; re-export `utils/__init__.py:13, 29`.
- **Evidence.** READ; whole-program search (DCP D13).

#### A-R35 — Level-2 client folder name · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does** (`create_client_state_key(client, state, max_length)`):
  1. whole words `Private` → `Pvt`, `Limited` → `Ltd`, any letter case, on the client text AS WRITTEN (spaces kept; underscores are NOT turned into spaces first, so `ClientA_Private_Limited` keeps its full words — A-R19);
  2. state code from A-R21 / A-R22;
  3. key = `"<client>-<state code>"`;
  4. if the key is longer than `max_length` (default **35**, A-R36): `available = max_length − len(code) − 1`; if `available > 5` the client part is cut to `available` characters (it may end with a space, e.g. `ClientA Pvt -DL`), otherwise the WHOLE key is cut to `max_length` characters and the state code is lost.
  No character cleaning is done here (the name comes from a valid file name, so only characters Windows allows in file names can arrive; `&`, `,`, `(`, `)`, `.`, `'`, `{`, `}`, `[`, `]`, `!`, `@`, `#`, `$`, `%`, `^`, `~`, `` ` ``, `+`, `=` and Unicode pass into the folder name — TRACE T2 obs 6). A client text starting with `_` gives a folder that Step 4 skips (A-R42).
- **Exact examples** (RAN me s2, s3):

| Client text | State text | Max | Folder name |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Beta Demo Industries Private Limited` | `Maharashtra` | 35 | `Beta Demo Industries Pvt Ltd-MH` |
| `Zeta_Private_Limited` | `Delhi` | 35 | `Zeta_Private_Limited-DL` |
| `Gamma Sample Manufacturing Company of India One` | `Maharashtra` | 35 | `Gamma Sample Manufacturing Compa-MH` (the "… Two" client gets the SAME folder) |
| `ClientA Private Limited` | `Delhi` | 15 | `ClientA Pvt -DL` (ends with a space before the hyphen) |
| `Beta Demo Industries` | `Maharashtra` | 9 | `Beta D-MH` |
| `Beta Demo Industries` | `Maharashtra` | 8 | `Beta Dem` (state code gone) |
| `_Alpha` | `Delhi` | 35 | `_Alpha-DL` (skipped by Step 4) |
| `ClientA` | `` (empty) | 35 | `ClientA-` |

  Consequences (RAN TRACE T6 §7, T8 §1; Windows part READ, MEDIUM): two different client keys share one Level-2 folder (and one Version folder) when they are abbreviation variants (`… Private Limited` / `… Pvt Ltd`), when their first `max − code − 1` characters agree (32 characters with a two-letter code at the default 35), or — on Windows, whose file names ignore letter case — when they differ only in capitals. For abbreviation variants the cleaned report names are identical too, so inside the shared Version folder the second client's reports replace the first's (A-R59; RAN VERIFY-A-S45 `v_abbrev_merge.py`). [GST RULE] because the state code is part of the name; nothing here is judged.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:620-644` (abbreviations `:624-625`, code `:628`, key `:631`, limit `:634-642`, the "> 5" test `:637`); used `core/file_organizer.py:83-87, 97-100`. The redundant `import re` inside the function is at `:623`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2, s3; TRACE T2, T6 §7, T9).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); unchanged (`utils/helpers.py:513-537@47a1492`).
- **Older documents.** `PROJECT_AUDIT_ANNUALSTATEMENT.md:511-514` says "minimum 5 chars"; the code needs MORE than 5 available characters and otherwise drops the state code (DOCS R12).

#### A-R36 — Maximum client folder name length values
- **What the code does.**
  - Default **35** (`tk.IntVar(value=35)`).
  - 0 or a negative number becomes 35 (organiser; restore from settings; arrow clicks).
  - The number box's arrows run from **15** to **100** (box width 5 characters).
  - Typed values are not checked: RAN (me s4) `IntVar.get()` gives `20` for `20.5`, `500` for `500`, `-3` for `-3`, and raises `TclError` for empty text or `abc` — then every settings save fails (A-R116) and a Step 3 run stops with "Fatal error" (`gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:194` is outside the per-client protection).
  - The hint next to the box reads `(default 35 · names over <limit> are shortened)`; if the value cannot be read the hint shows 35.
  - The value limits ONLY the Level-2 folder name (A-R35). The Level-4 suffix (A-R39), the copied file names (A-R48) and the report names (A-R59) keep the full cleaned client name (RAN CORE exp4, TRACE T2). The help text says "Longest client name allowed in a folder name before it is shortened" (`gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:119-123`).

- **What the code does.** Defined, never used; Level 2 is built by A-R35 instead (with the state CODE, not the state text this template suggests).

#### A-R38 — Version folder
- **What the code does.** Inside the client folder a folder `Version-{timestamp}` (A-R33, 19 characters, e.g. `Version-211026 1030`) is created for EVERY run in EVERY mode. A same-minute run reuses it (exist is fine). Because the name is always the current minute, "Resume" never re-enters the Version folder of an earlier, interrupted run (A-R43).
- **Where.** Template `utils/constants.py:84`; built `core/file_organizer.py:102-108`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6 §5 run 5: a Resume one minute later made `Version-211026 1045` and copied everything again).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).

#### A-R39 — Level-4 category folders
- **What the code does.** Three folders are created in every Version folder, even when empty: templates `GSTR3B ({client})` (key `gstr3b`), `ITC ({client})` (key `itc`), `Sales ({client})` (key `sales`). `{client}` is `sanitize_filename(client)` — the FULL cleaned name with underscores (A-R20), never shortened by A-R36. When the client name is not to be included (A-R40) the text ` (<client>)` is removed by a plain text replace, leaving `GSTR3B`, `ITC`, `Sales`. The code comment records the reason for the short names: on a real run of 3030 files the old long names gave a worst path of 235 characters with 27 files over Excel's ~218-character limit; the short names gave 215 with none over ⚠ (the 218 figure is Excel behaviour, A-R104).
- **Where.** `utils/constants.py:85-95` (comment `:85-90`, names `:92-94`); `core/file_organizer.py:90` (cleaned client), `:117-139` (loop; replace at `:133-134`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s1: `GSTR3B/`, `ITC/`, `Sales/`; TRACE T6 §6: with the name on, `ITC (Gamma_Sample_Enterprises_LLP)`-style names).
- **History.** Short names since `7347383` (A only (after the split)). At the split they were `GSTR-3B Exports ({client})`, `Other ITC related files ({client})`, `Sales related files ({client})` (`utils/constants.py:86-88@47a1492`, shared - before the split on 30-10-2025). Folders made by older runs keep the old names.
- **Older documents.** `README.md:185-187` and `PROJECT_AUDIT_ANNUALSTATEMENT.md:518-527` still show the long names (DOCS R13). The help text `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:112-117` gives the example `'ITC (ABC Pvt Ltd)'` with spaces; the real folder has underscores (`ITC (ABC_Pvt_Ltd)`, RAN TRACE T6 §6).
- **Not a GST rule** (registry); which return file lands in which of these folders is A-R41.

#### A-R40 — Include-client-name decision
- **What the code does.** If the global tick "Include client name in Level 4 folder names" is on, every client's three Level-4 folders carry the name. Otherwise the per-client flag stored under key `"<client as written>-<state code>"` decides (default off). The global tick is read once at the start of a run; the per-client dictionary is handed over by reference, so a toggle during a run affects later clients. Every scan resets all per-client flags to off (A-R137).
- **Where.** `core/file_organizer.py:121-134` (key `:122`, global `:125-127`, per-client `:128-130`); flags handed over `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:192-193`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6 §6 runs 7-8).
- **History.** Per-client key by state CODE since `33d0baf` (A only (after the split)); before it the GUI stored flags under the state NAME (`gui/handlers/client_handler.py:178@47a1492`, `:214@47a1492`, shared - before the split on 30-10-2025) while the organiser looked them up by CODE, so per-client flags were silently ignored.
- **Not a GST rule.**

| File type | Folder key | Real folder |

| `GSTR-2B Reco` | `itc` | `ITC[ (<client>)]` |

| `Sales Reco` | `sales` | `Sales[ (<client>)]` |
| `Annual Report` | `version` | the Version folder itself (comment "Goes in version folder") |

#### A-R42 — Step 4: which run and which version are used
- **What the code does.**
  1. Inside the folder in Step 4's box, every entry matching `Annual Statement-*` is collected — FILES included (no folder test). None → log "No Annual Statement folders found" (warning), status "No processed folders found", and the PREVIOUS client list and run folder stay as they were.
  2. The entry with the newest date modified is the run folder; it is stored as `self.level1_folder` and later receives the consolidated workbook (A-R86). If that newest entry is a FILE, listing it fails and the scan ends with "Error scanning folder" (RAN PQ).
  3. Every sub-folder of it whose name does not start with `_` is a client; inside it every `Version-*` entry is collected (files too) and the newest by date modified is used. Client folders with no `Version-*` are left out without a message.
  4. For that Version folder, `has_itc` / `has_sales` = some file name (any letter case) starts with `itc_report_` / `sales_report_` and ends with `.xlsx` (refreshed copies count, A-R59).
  5. Clients appear in the order the file system lists the folders (no sorting; see UNREGISTERED U3 in 5.18).
  6. Pointing the box AT an `Annual Statement-…` folder finds nothing (it looks for `Annual Statement-*` INSIDE the chosen folder).
  Writing any file into an older run folder or Version folder (for example Excel's `~$` lock file when someone opens a report there) makes that older folder "newest" (RAN PQ scan probe, TRACE T3c on Linux; that Windows updates folder dates the same way is MEDIUM).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:611-668` (empty box `:613-615`; glob `:622-623`; none found `:625-628`; newest `:631`; stored `:632`; clients `:638-639`; versions `:641-648`; classify `:651` → `:20-42`; record `:653-659`; error `:665-668`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (PQ scan probes; TRACE T3c, T6 §8; EXT t14).
- **History.** Version by date modified since `c1583d6` (A only (after the split)); before it by NAME (`power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:529@47a1492`, shared - before the split on 30-10-2025), which picked the wrong folder across month boundaries.
- **Older documents.** The design note (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-merge-extractor-design.md:85-89`) and the `bfcb24a` commit message say pointing at an older `Annual Statement-…` folder "still works"; the code does not support that (RAN EXT t14, PQ).
- **Not a GST rule** (it decides WHICH data set is reported; technical).

### 5.8 Category 7 — Copying and versioning of files (A-R43 to A-R50)

#### A-R43 — Processing modes
- **What the code does.** Three modes, keyed exactly (case-sensitive):

| Key | Name shown | Description shown | Help (tooltip) | Level-1 folder | Version folder | Destination file already there |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fresh` (default) | `🆕 Fresh Run` | `Create new folder structure (recommended for first time)` | "Create a brand new timestamped folder for this run. / Use this normally - previous runs are left alone." | new `Annual Statement-<now>` (A-R32) | new | backup, then overwrite (A-R45) |
| `rerun` | `🔄 Re-Run` | `Add new version folders to existing structure` | "Add a new version inside the most recent run's folder instead of starting a new one. / Use when redoing work for the same batch." | the existing `Annual Statement-*` FOLDER with the newest date modified; a new one if none | new | overwrite, no backup |
| `resume` | `▶️ Resume` | `Continue previously interrupted processing` | "Continue a run that was interrupted, skipping files that are already in place." | as Re-run | new | skipped ("File already exists") |

  Any other mode text stops processing with `ValueError("Invalid processing mode: <mode>")` (RAN TRACE T8 §3: `Fresh` and `dry` refused). Re-run/Resume consider only FOLDERS whose name starts with `Annual Statement-` (unlike Step 4, A-R42) and pick the newest by date modified; the result is worked out once per run (A-R111). Because the Version folder is always new (A-R38), Resume skips files only when it starts in the same minute as the interrupted run; otherwise it copies everything again into a new Version folder (RAN TRACE T6 runs 4-5, EXT t12). The processing-mode radio buttons have no save command, so a changed mode is remembered only if some other save happens (A-R116).
- **Where.** Definitions `utils/constants.py:174-190`; default `gui/main_window.py:87`; check `core/file_organizer.py:60-63`; Level-1 choice `:163-196` (folders only `:177-180`, newest `:184`); Version `:102-108`; destination handling `:276-287`; radios `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:401-412`; help `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:103-110`; read once per run `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:191, 219`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6 §5, T8 §3; EXT t12).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Older documents.** `PROJECT_AUDIT_ANNUALSTATEMENT.md:559-562` describes resume as "skip existing files" without the same-minute limitation (DOCS R17); the Step 1 help text "Each run adds a new timestamped folder, so previous runs are left untouched" (`gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:83-85`) is true only for Fresh runs in a new minute.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R44 — `PROCESSING_MODES[*]['icon']` · DEAD
- **What the code does.** `'🆕'`, `'🔄'`, `'▶️'` — never read (the same emoji are typed inside the `name` texts).

#### A-R45 — A destination file that already exists
- **What the code does.** Checked per copied file:
  - Fresh → `create_backup` copies the EXISTING destination file to a backup name (A-R46), the result records `backup`, then the new copy overwrites the destination; if the backup fails (returns nothing) the copy overwrites anyway, without a message beyond the log.
  - Resume → not copied; status `Skipped`, error `File already exists`.
  - Re-run → no branch: overwritten without a backup.
- **Where.** `core/file_organizer.py:276-287` (Fresh `:278-282`, Resume `:283-287`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6 runs 2-4).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R46 — Backup name
- **What the code does.** `<stem>_backup_<DDMMYY_HHMM><suffix>` in the SAME folder as the file being replaced, with the clock time of the backup (not the run stamp). A second backup of the same file in the same minute overwrites the first (RAN TRACE T8 §4: "v1 lost"). Backups therefore sit inside the category folders that the reports link to (the GSTR-3B folder is linked as a whole folder, A-R54 B2) — whether a template query reads them depends on the template (MEDIUM; not visible in the repository).
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:217-234` (name `:224-226`, copy `:228`, log `:229`); stamp `utils/helpers.py:33-35`; caller `core/file_organizer.py:280`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T8 §4, T6 run 2).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Note.** The dead `EXCEL_SAFETY['backup_prefix'] = 'backup_'` (A-R107) and the `.gitignore` entry `backup_*` describe a PREFIX; real backups use the INFIX `_backup_` (EXT §4.7.5).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R47 — Copy method
- **What the code does.**
  1. The source must exist and be a file, else the copy fails (`Source file not found: …` in the log).
  2. The destination folder is created if needed.
  3. `shutil.copy2` copies the content AND the dates (the copy keeps the original's date modified).
  4. With `verify=True` (always, from the organiser) the two sizes are compared; on a mismatch the copy is DELETED and the copy fails (in Re-run mode the previous good copy at that path was already overwritten).
  5. Any error → failure; a half-written destination (e.g. after a network drop) is NOT removed.
  6. Copying a file onto itself fails (the `SameFileError` is caught).
  7. Success logs `Successfully copied <name> to <full destination>` at INFO.
  Source files are only read: never moved, renamed or deleted (RAN TRACE T6: names, checksums, sizes and dates to the nanosecond unchanged after nine runs).
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:172-215` (source check `:191-193`, folder `:196`, copy `:199`, size check `:202-208`, log `:210`, error `:213-215`); caller `core/file_organizer.py:290`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6; CORE exp4).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R48 — Names of the copies and the in-memory rename
- **What the code does.** Each copy is named `sanitize_filename(original file name)` (A-R20), e.g. `GSTR3B-Alpha Test Traders-Maharashtra-Apr.xlsx` → `GSTR3B-Alpha_Test_Traders-Maharashtra-Apr.xlsx`, `… 31032025 (1).xlsx` → `…31032025_(1).xlsx`, `(1) GSTR3B-…` → `(1)_GSTR3B-…` (RAN me s1, s3). After a SUCCESSFUL copy the client's file record gets `name` and `sanitized_name` = the copy's name; these names are what the Links cells later receive (A-R54 to A-R57). `Skipped` or `Failed` files keep the ORIGINAL name, so a same-minute Resume links names that do not exist on disk (RAN CORE exp8, EXT t12). The records are the same objects as the scan results (`app.scanned_files`, `app.client_data`), so after a run the original names are gone from memory until the next scan (Dry Run then shows cleaned names). Two originals that clean to the same name (e.g. `…-Apr-Mar.xlsx` and `…-Apr-Mar .xlsx`, or a `Private Limited` and a `Pvt Ltd` spelling of one client) overwrite or skip each other; both are reported "Success" (RAN TRACE T8 §1-2).
- **Where.** `core/file_organizer.py:271-274` (name), `:233-246` (rename after Success); organisation report uses `sanitized_name` when present `core/file_organizer.py:359`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (CORE exp8; EXT t12; TRACE T8).
- **Not a GST rule.**

### 5.9 Category 8 — Report creation and template Links cells (A-R51 to A-R63)

#### A-R51 — Processing needs both templates and a target · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** Before Dry Run or Start, in this order:
  1. both template paths set, else `Please select both ITC and Sales template files`;
  2. target set, else `Please select a target folder for organized files`;
  3. ITC template exists, else `ITC template file not found`;
  4. Sales template exists, else `Sales template file not found`.
  The target need not exist (it is created when processing starts: `ValueError("Cannot create target folder: …")` if that fails). Neither the template's file type nor its content (Links sheet, queries) is checked here. The Step 1 checklist is stricter (it wants the target to exist and the template suffix to be in its list, A-R133) but only colours the readiness pill; it does not block Start.
- **Where.** `gui/handlers/file_handler.py:132-151` (texts `utils/constants.py:211-212` for 1-2; literal texts `:144, :148`); callers `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:40` (Dry Run), `:78` (Start); target creation `core/file_organizer.py:65-69`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T7: a missing target passes; a `.txt` "template" passes).
- **Note.** Tagged [GST RULE] conservatively (registry): it gates whether returns are processed at all.

#### A-R56 — Form of the Links values · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.**
  - Folder values: the full path exactly as chosen in Step 1 (a mapped drive letter or a `\\server\share` path), passed through `str(Path(...))` with every `/` turned into `\` (`clean_windows_path`; on Windows the `Path` step already gives backslashes).
  - File values: the cleaned copy name (A-R48) WITHOUT its extension (`Path(name).stem`), so the template must add the extension itself; an `.xls` input is linked by its stem exactly like an `.xlsx` (whether the template then finds it depends on the template — MEDIUM).
  - Every value is written as text (`str(value)`). Through Excel/COM, Excel may still turn a number-looking text into a number (LOW; only if a stem looks numeric; how to check: write a digits-only stem through COM and inspect the cell).
  - An EMPTY value is not written at all, so the template's existing cell content stays.
  - An unknown report type gets an empty mapping and no cells are written.
  - Each written value is logged at INFO with its first 50 characters (`  B2 = <value>...`), so folder paths and client names reach the log (A-R122).
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:418-422` (`clean_windows_path`), `:553-555` (`extract_filename_without_extension`); `core/excel_handler.py:191-195` (COM: get value, skip empty, write `str(value)`, log), `:299-301` (openpyxl); unknown type `core/excel_handler.py:162, 285` (empty mapping).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2: `clean_windows_path` → `Z:\Clients\Annual Statement-211026 1030\ITC`; EXT t7: placeholders kept; on Linux the converted paths look like `\root\…`, which is meaningless there — TRACE T6).
- **Note.** The values are ABSOLUTE paths. A report built through `Z:` refreshes on another PC only if that PC reaches the share by the same letter (MEDIUM; depends on how the template's queries use the cells).

#### A-R57 — Which file of a type feeds the report: the first in sorted full-path order · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** The scan sorts all files by their full path (Python `Path` ordering: ignores letter case on Windows, respects it on Linux). Each Links file value takes element `[0]` of that type's list, i.e. the alphabetically first. Examples (RAN TRACE T3 (b), me s1):

| Two files of one type | The one named in the report |
|---|---|

Creating one...`; a new sheet is added, renamed `Links`, filled — and the report counts as SUCCESS |

#### A-R59 — Report file names · [GST RULE]

Private Limited_MH_211026_1030.xlsx`, RAN TRACE T2), `<state code>` = the stored code (else recomputed), `<stamp>` = the run stamp with `_` for the space (`211026_1030`).

  - Two clients whose names are abbreviation variants (`<Client A> Private Limited` and `<Client A> Pvt Ltd`, same state) get the SAME `<safe client>` (`ClientA_Pvt_Ltd` for the invented `ClientA`) and therefore the same report names on every operating system; since they also share one Version folder (A-R35), the second client's ITC and Sales reports silently replace the first's, and the run still logs `✅ Success` four times and "Reports created: 4" (RAN VERIFY-A-S45 `v_abbrev_merge.py`: one ITC and one Sales report on disk, two organisation text reports).

#### A-R60 — Step 3 order per client and how success is counted · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** For each ticked client, in the order of the client table (5.18 U3):
  1. stop requested? → leave the loop (Stop acts only between clients);
  2. `client_info = app.client_data[key]` — OUTSIDE the per-client protection, so a key that is not there (the `0042` → `42` case of A-R17, or a Re-scan during the run) ends the WHOLE run with `💥 Fatal error: '<key>'` and no summary workbook (RAN TRACE T8 §2);
  3. progress and ETA (A-R141);
  4. folders (A-R32 to A-R40) → copies (A-R45 to A-R48) → ITC report → Sales report → organisation text report (A-R62);
  5. BOTH reports are attempted for every client whatever files it has;
  6. ITC: success → reports +1, ITC +1, log `✅ Success: <name>.xlsx`; `False` → "Report Creation Errors" +1 and `❌ Failed to create ITC report` / `Check if template has 'Links' sheet`; an exception (including a template that vanished, `FileNotFoundError` from `core/excel_handler.py:82-84`) → errors +1, `❌ ITC Report Error: <text>`;
  7. Sales: the template path is re-read; empty → `❌ Sales template path is empty!`; missing on disk → `❌ Sales template file not found at: <path>`; then as ITC, with extra on-screen log lines starting `[DEBUG]` — the Sales branch holds 14 different `[DEBUG]` texts (`gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:333-411`), of which 8 appear per client on a normal success (`:333, 342, 362, 363, 367, 371, 374, 391`) and between 1 and 10 depending on the path taken (READ, HIGH) [Corrected during verification — originally "14 extra lines" per client];
  8. the client counts as "Success" unless an error escapes steps 4-7 — a client whose two reports both failed is still "Success";
  9. an error inside the protected part marks the client failed (`Errors` sheet row) and the run goes on.
  Read live during the run: the ITC and Sales template paths (changing them mid-run changes later clients) and the per-client name flags; read once at the start: target, mode, global name tick, length.
- **Where.** `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:228-453` (loop `:228`; stop `:229-230`; lookup `:232`; protected part from `:243`; folders `:253`; copies `:271`; ITC `:296-326`; Sales `:328-411`; text report `:414`; success `:416-438`; failure `:442-453`); live reads `:193, 301, 310, 332`; once `:190-194, 219-225`; outer handler `:522-525`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T6, T8 §2).
- **Note.** Tagged [GST RULE] conservatively (registry): it decides that every client gets both reports.

#### A-R61 — Step 3 summary workbook
- **What the code does.** Written only if at least one client was processed and a Level-1 folder exists, as `GST_Processing_Summary_<DDMMYY_HHMM>.xlsx` in the Level-1 folder, saved straight to its final place with openpyxl (no staging; a same-minute second run overwrites it and lists only its own clients — RAN TRACE T6 run 2). Five sheets, in this order:

| Sheet | Content |
|---|---|
| `Summary` | A1 `GST FILE PROCESSING SUMMARY` (bold 16, merged A1:B1); from row 3, label in A (bold) and value in B: `Processing Date` (the run stamp TEXT, e.g. `211026 1030`), `Total Clients`, `Successful`, `Failed`, `Total Files` (successful copies), `Reports Generated`, `ITC Reports Created`, `Sales Reports Created`, `Report Creation Errors`, `Processing Mode`, `Target Folder`, `Include Client Name in Folders` (`Yes`/`No`) |
| `Client Status` | headers `Client`, `State`, `Status`, `Files`, `Missing`, `Complete %`; one row per SUCCESSFUL client: client and state as written, `Success`, file count, NUMBER of missing types, completeness as TEXT `66.7%` |
| `File Mapping` | headers `Original File`, `Client`, `Type`, `Destination`, `Status`; one row per file of each successful client (original name, client KEY, type, full destination path, `Success`/`Failed`/`Skipped`) |
| `Errors` | headers `Time`, `Client`, `Type`, `Error`, `Action`; one row per failed client (`%H:%M:%S`, key, `Processing Error`, message, `Review`), or `No errors encountered` merged A2:E2 |
| `Variations` | headers `Filename`, `Issue`, `Expected Pattern`, `Size`, `Action`; one row per variation (size in bytes, `Rename file`), or `No file variations found` merged A2:E2 |

  Headers: bold white (FFFFFF) size 12 on solid fill `366092`, centred, thin borders; data cells: thin borders. Column width = min(longest text + 2, 50). The per-client flags `itc_report_created` / `sales_report_created` are collected but never written. A failure only logs `❌ Failed to create summary report` / `❌ Summary Report Error: …`.
- **Where.** `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:455-475` (condition `:456`, name `:462-464`, call `:469`); data `:209-226, 417-451`; `core/excel_handler.py:371-601` (sheets `:391-395`; styles `:411-430`; Summary `:432-462`; Client Status `:464-491`; File Mapping `:493-519`; Errors `:521-552`; Variations `:554-585`; widths `:587-601`; save `:402`).

  So "Successfully Copied" and "Folders Created" are wrong for every client after the first (RAN TRACE T6: the second client's report said 18 / 21 for 2 files). It is written even if copies failed; a write error is ignored (returns nothing, the caller does not look).
