### 5.10 Category 9 — Power Query refresh (A-R64 to A-R73)

#### A-R64 — Step 4 needs pywin32; there is no fallback
- **What the code does.** The five pywin32 modules (`pythoncom`, `win32com.client`, `win32api`, `win32con`, `win32gui`) are imported inside a guard; if any is missing, a warning is logged at import time (`pywin32 not available (<reason>). Power Query refresh is disabled; install it with: pip install pywin32`) and, for every client, BOTH reports get the status `{success: False, error: "pywin32 is not installed for this Python, so Power Query cannot be refreshed. Install it with: pip install pywin32"}` without looking at the files. The run still writes a consolidated workbook full of `✗` and `Missing` and offers to open it.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:20-36` (guard), `:66-77` (refusal); consolidated anyway `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:941-955`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T4 Part C; me s3/s4: the import-time warning printed on this Linux machine).
- **History.** Guard added in `c463256` (A only (after the split)); before it the import failed outright.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R65 — Which reports are processed for a client
- **What the code does.** Per client row there is an ITC and a Sales tick; each starts ticked and enabled only where that report exists (A-R42 step 4), disabled otherwise. Only clients with at least one tick are sent (as a shallow copy of the row's data plus `process_itc` / `process_sales`). Nothing ticked → `No Selection` / `Please select at least one report to process`. An unticked report gets `{success: False, error: 'Not selected for processing'}`. A missing flag counts as ticked ("for backward compatibility"). Buttons: `✓ Select All` (both ticks where the report exists), `✗ Deselect All` (both off), `ITC Only` (ITC where it exists, Sales off), `Sales Only` (ITC off, Sales where it exists).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:693-694` (initial ticks), `:720-737` (enabled only if present), `:845-861` (collect, none selected), `:810-836` (buttons); `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:86-104`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (PQ gui_probe).
- **History.** Per-report ticks since `bd176bf` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R66 — Which file is the "original" report
- **What the code does.** In the client's chosen Version folder the search `ITC_Report_*.xlsx` / `Sales_Report_*.xlsx` is run and the FIRST file the file system lists is taken as the original. Earlier refreshed copies match the same search, so where the file system does not list in name order the copy of a copy is refreshed (RAN TRACE T5 / XL E4 on Linux: `…_Refreshed_211026_1040_Refreshed_211026_0422.xlsx`). On a Windows NTFS disk names are normally listed in sorted order and `….xlsx` sorts before `…_Refreshed_…` (a dot before an underscore), so the plain original usually comes first there (MEDIUM; NAS shares may differ). None found → `No ITC report found` / `No Sales report found`. When two clients share a folder (A-R35), only one report of each kind is used (RAN TRACE T9).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:111-125` (search `:112-113`, none `:115-123`, first `:125`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T5, T9; XL E4).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R67 — Refreshed-copy name
- **What the code does.** `<original stem><suffix>.xlsx` in the same folder, where suffix = the "Refreshed File Suffix" text with the exact lower-case `{timestamp}` replaced by `%d%m%y_%H%M` (default `_Refreshed_{timestamp}` → `…_Refreshed_211026_1100.xlsx`, 22 characters longer than the original). The copy is made with `shutil.copy2`, so it keeps the ORIGINAL's date modified until Excel saves it; a same-minute copy of the same report is silently overwritten. RAN (me s3; TRACE T5): an empty suffix makes the copy's name equal the original's → `… are the same file`, report failed, nothing extracted; `{TIMESTAMP}` in capitals stays literally in the name (`…_Refreshed_{TIMESTAMP}.xlsx`, overwritten every run); a suffix without `{timestamp}` (e.g. `_Refreshed`) writes the same name every run and can later be picked as the "original" (`_Refreshed_Refreshed`); a suffix with `/` fails (`FileNotFoundError`, RAN PQ). The suffix text is not validated for characters Windows forbids.
- **Default typed three times:** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:51`; `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:297`; `power_query_extractor/core/refresh_naming.py:14` (`DEFAULT_SUFFIX_PATTERN`, imported but unused at `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:38`).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:127-130` (stamp `:128`, replace `:129`, name `:130`), `:162-164` (copy); value taken from the box at Start `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:865`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s3; TRACE T5; PQ).
- **History.** Configurable since `bd176bf` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R68 — Recognising a refreshed file
- **What the code does.**
  - Markers = the non-empty pieces of the LOWER-CASED suffix pattern split at `{timestamp}`; none (empty pattern, or `{timestamp}` alone) → the legacy marker `['_refreshed_']`.
  - A name is "refreshed" if, lower-cased, it ends in `.xlsx` AND (it contains ALL markers anywhere, OR it contains `_refreshed_`).
  - A "refreshed copy of" an original additionally starts with the original's stem (any case).
  RAN (me s3; TRACE T5):

| Suffix pattern | Markers | Copy recognised? | Plain original looks refreshed? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_Refreshed_{timestamp}` | `_refreshed_` | yes | no |
| `_Done_{timestamp}` | `_done_` | yes | no |
| `_R{timestamp}` | `_r` | yes | **yes** (`_r` is inside `itc_report_`) |
| `_{timestamp}` | `_` | yes | **yes** |
| `{timestamp}` | `_refreshed_` (legacy) | **no** (the copy has no `_refreshed_`) | no |
| `_Refreshed_{TIMESTAMP}` | `_refreshed_` | yes (literal `{TIMESTAMP}` in the name) | no |

  Also: an original whose cleaned client name contains `Refreshed` looks refreshed under every pattern; with `_v{timestamp}` any client starting with V does; Excel's `~$…` lock files pass the name test but are excluded by the callers' prefix tests. The pieces are not checked in order or position.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/refresh_naming.py:14-66` (`TIMESTAMP_TOKEN` `:15`, `LEGACY_MARKER` `:20`, `refresh_markers` `:23-40`, `is_refreshed_name` `:43-55`, `is_refreshed_copy_of` `:58-66`); used `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:64` and `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:142-146`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s3; TRACE T5; registry check2.py).
- **History.** Added in `2aaa2b1` (A only (after the split)); before it both readers hard-coded `_Refreshed_` / `_refreshed_`.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R69 — Skip refresh
- **What the code does.** When "Skip Refresh (Use existing refreshed files)" is ticked, for each report: all files `<original stem>*.xlsx` in the Version folder that pass A-R68's "copy of" test are candidates; the newest by date modified is used WITHOUT refreshing, with status `{success: True, error: None, validation_message: 'Skipped - Using existing refreshed file'}` and the log line `Skip refresh enabled - using existing: <name>`; if there is none, `No existing refreshed file found - will refresh` and the report is refreshed normally. Nothing checks that the reused copy was ever successfully refreshed: a copy left by a FAILED refresh (dated like the original, A-R67) is reused and reported as a success (RAN TRACE T10). The tick writes the process-wide option `EXTRACTION_OPTIONS['skip_refresh']` (file default `False`), which is read per report, so ticking it during a run affects later reports; it always starts unticked and is not saved (A-R119). The 3-second wait after each report (A-R72) also happens in Skip mode.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:132-159` (read option `:134-135`, candidates `:142-146`, newest `:149`, status `:151-155`, none `:156-159`); tick `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:315-325, 483-492`; default `power_query_extractor/config/cell_mappings.py:249`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T5, T10).
- **History.** Since `bd176bf` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); configured-suffix matching since `2aaa2b1` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R70 — `EXTRACTION_OPTIONS['auto_refresh_if_missing'] = True` · DEAD
- **What the code does.** Comment "Automatically refresh if no refreshed file found"; never read — the behaviour is hard-coded (A-R69, `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:156-159`), so setting it to `False` would change nothing.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/config/cell_mappings.py:250`.

#### A-R71 — How the refresh is done: key presses · ⚠
- **What the code does** (one visible Excel session per report):
  1. `CoInitialize`; `Dispatch("Excel.Application")` (may attach to an Excel already running — MEDIUM).
  2. Up to 5 tries, 2 s apart: `Visible = True` ("Keep it visible to avoid issues"), `DisplayAlerts = False`, `WindowState = -4137` (maximised); after 5 failures it carries on.
  3. Open the refreshed COPY (A-R102), up to 3 tries 3 s apart, only when the error text contains `Call was rejected by callee`; after three such failures it carries on with no workbook and fails later at Save.
  4. Wait 5 × 1 s ("Loading... i/5 seconds").
  5. `WScript.Shell.AppActivate(excel.Caption)` up to 3 times, 1 s apart; if it never succeeds a warning is logged and it CARRIES ON — the keys then go to whatever window has the focus; then 1 s.
  6. `SendKeys("%a")` (Alt+A), 1 s, `SendKeys("r")`, 1 s, `SendKeys("a")` — in English Excel this is Data → Refresh All ⚠.
  7. Wait `wait_time` × 1 s; error-dialog checks (A-R105); extended wait `max(10, wait_time)` × 1 s; final dialog check.
  8. `Info!BB2` check (A-R73); `Save()` ALWAYS (even after an error dialog or a failed check); 2 s; `Close(SaveChanges=True)`; 1 s.
  9. `finally`: `Close(SaveChanges=False)` (usually already closed — error swallowed), `Quit()`, `CoUninitialize()` (each error swallowed).
  There is no `RefreshAll`, no `BackgroundQuery = False`, no `CalculateUntilAsyncQueriesDone`, no "still refreshing" test anywhere (READ, search). A `return {'success': True, 'error': None}` at `:383-386` can never be reached.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:209-415` (Dispatch `:218`; properties `:221-234`; open `:236-249`; load wait `:251-255`; activation `:258-276`; keys `:278-293`; wait `:297-302`; dialogs `:305-322`; extended `:324-330`; final `:332-342`; check `:345`; save `:348-351`; status `:354-357`; close `:360-363`; result `:365-374`; inner error `:376-381`; unreachable `:383-386`; outer error `:388-393`; clean-up `:394-415`).
- **Evidence.** READ only (COM and key presses cannot run on Linux).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); the "Increased from 0.5" / "Increased from 3 to 5" comments (`:253, 283, 288`) record earlier, shorter waits.
- **⚠** Ribbon key tips depend on the Excel version and the UI language; the window title used for activation depends on Excel.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R72 — Refresh waits and retries
- **What the code does.** Every fixed wait and retry (W = the "Power Query Wait Time" box):

| Step | Value | Where (`power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py`) |
|---|---|---|
| Default W | 10 s | `:50`; box `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:270` |
| Box range (arrows) | 5 to 60; hint `seconds (Min: 5, Max: 60)` | `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:271-287` |
| Typed W | not checked: `0` accepted, `12.7` → 12, `abc`/empty → `TclError` and the Start click silently does nothing (RAN PQ gui_probe) | `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:864` |
| Excel property retries | 5 tries, 2 s apart | `:222-232` |
| Open retries (only on "Call was rejected by callee") | 3 tries, 3 s apart | `:239-247` |
| Load wait | 5 × 1 s | `:253-255` |
| Window activation | 3 tries, 1 s after each failure; then 1 s | `:264-276` |
| Between keys | 1 s, 1 s | `:283, 288` |
| Refresh wait | W × 1 s | `:300-302` |
| Error-dialog checks | 5 checks, 1 s apart (stop at the first found; closing waits 0.5 s) | `:310-319`, `:522` |
| Extended wait | max(10, W) × 1 s (logged every 2 s) | `:326-330` |
| After save / after close | 2 s / 1 s | `:351`, `:363` |
| Before extraction ("Waiting for file to be released") | 3 s, also in Skip mode | `:183-185` |
| Second open attempt (close same-named workbooks) | 3 s | `:750` |
| Third open attempt | 2 s | `:758` |

  Fixed waiting per refreshed report when nothing fails = 5 + 1 + 2 + W + 5 + max(10, W) + 2 + 1 = **16 + W + max(10, W)** seconds, plus 3 s before extraction: W = 5 → 31 + 3 s; W = 10 → **36 + 3 s**; W = 60 → 136 + 3 s (RAN me s2, arithmetic over the code's own constants), plus Excel start/open times, twice per client with both reports.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s2; PQ gui_probe for typed values).
- **History.** Configurable wait since `bd176bf` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R73 — When a refresh counts as successful · [GST RULE] · ⚠
- **What the code does.** A refreshed report's status is `success: True` only if ALL hold:
  1. no error dialog was found (A-R105) during the 5 checks or the final check;
  2. a sheet named `info` (any letter case) exists — else "Info sheet not found";
  3. its cell `BB2` is not `None` ("Cell BB2 is empty"), not blank after trimming ("Cell BB2 is blank"), not `0` / `0.0` ("Cell BB2 contains 0"), and its upper-cased text is not one of `#REF!`, `#VALUE!`, `#NAME?`, `#NULL!`, `#DIV/0!`, `#N/A`, `#NUM!`, `#ERROR!` ("Cell BB2 contains error: …");
  4. no error was raised on the way.
  Success message: `Validated - Client Name: <BB2>` (the code comment says BB2 holds the client name). A failed check sets the error text `Refresh validation failed: <reason>`, which REPLACES a dialog's text if both happened. The check cannot tell a value freshly loaded by the refresh from one already in the file (READ, HIGH). A COM error cell probably arrives as a large negative number such as `-2146826246`, which passes the check (RAN with fake sheets; that COM returns that number is MEDIUM). Whatever the status, the cells are read afterwards (A-R83), and a non-success status later turns every figure of that report into "Missing" (A-R89).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:638-685` (sheet `:645-648`, cell `:656`, checks `:659-678`, list `:675`, message `:681`); combined `:344-374`; extraction regardless `:187-188`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (PQ: `_validate_refresh` with fake sheets — `None`, `''`, `'   '`, `0`, `0.0`, `'0'`, `'#N/A'`, `'#n/a'` fail; `'SomeName'`, `12.5`, `-2146826246` pass). RAN VERIFY-A-S45 (`v_validate.py`): the same results, and also `True` and `False` (a TRUE/FALSE cell) PASS (`Validated - Client Name: False`), and a workbook whose only sheet is not `info` gives "Info sheet not found".
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **⚠** The sheet name and cell position follow the template layout. [GST RULE] conservatively: it decides whether a report's figures appear.

### 5.11 Category 10 — Extraction cell mappings (A-R74 to A-R85)

All extraction mappings live in one dictionary, `EXTRACTION_CONFIG` (`power_query_extractor/config/cell_mappings.py:33-242`), one entry per output sheet of the consolidated workbook. Each mapping names the report it reads (`report_type`), the sheet inside that report (`input_sheet`), one cell (`input_cell`), the column title in the output (`output_column`) and a `calculation` (always `None` today). The file is byte-identical in projects A and B; it has not changed since `47a1492` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025). **[GST RULE] for every row** — which report, sheet and cell a figure is taken from, and under which return or table label it is reported, is the author's decision; nothing here says whether any row is right. **⚠ for every row** — return and table names (GSTR-3B, GSTR-1, GSTR-2B, IMS, GSTR-9 Tables 6J/8D), sheet names and cell addresses follow the template version of the time.

The cause is the lookup of A-R88. Which cell belongs in each column is the author's call.

#### A-R83 — How the mappings are read · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.**
  1. **At load time** (when the module is imported): for each output sheet and each mapping that has BOTH an input sheet and an input cell, the field name `"<output sheet>_<output column>"` with every space turned into `_` is filed as `CELL_MAPPINGS[report type][input sheet][input cell] = field name`. A second mapping of the same cell for the same report type and sheet would replace the first, so the first output column would always show "Missing" (RAN TRACE T4b; not the case today). A missing key in any entry stops the module (and Step 4) from loading. "Backward compatibility" in the comment is misleading: this IS the structure the extractor uses.
  2. **At extraction** (A-F23): for each input sheet of the report type, the sheet is looked for by EXACT name, capitals and spaces included (`diff - gstr-1` is not `Diff - GSTR-1`, RAN TRACE T4); missing → each of its fields is stored as `None` (the bare cell keys are not added) and a warning is logged; found → each cell's `Range(cell).Value` is stored TWICE, under the field name and under the bare cell reference (e.g. `IMS_Reco_IGST` and `AA1`); a cell that cannot be read → both `None`; any other error with the sheet → its fields `None`. Every value is logged (`✓ <field> (<cell>) = <value>`) and listed again in an "Extraction summary" (A-R122). The count "Total values extracted: n" counts both keys, so it is about twice the number of cells. Any error outside the sheet loop → an empty result `{}` (the report's status from the refresh is unchanged, so a ✓ row can show only "Missing", A-R89).
  3. The extraction opens the copy in a second, HIDDEN Excel (`Dispatch`, `Visible = False`, `DisplayAlerts = False`); the log says "Opening file in read-only mode" / "✓ File opened in read-only mode" but the file is opened normally, not read-only (READ, HIGH); it is closed without saving.
- **Where.** Builder `power_query_extractor/config/cell_mappings.py:260-278` (condition `:270`, field name `:276`, filing `:277`); extraction `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:527-636` (hidden Excel `:533-539`, "read-only" texts `:541-543`, mappings `:546`, exact sheet `:564-568`, missing sheet `:570-576`, two keys `:584-588`, cell error `:591-594`, sheet error `:596-601`, summary log `:603-611`, `{}` on error `:615-617`, clean-up `:618-636`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T4 Part B2 through an openpyxl-backed stand-in for COM — it can only show values cached in the file, so a never-calculated formula reads as empty there while real Excel would compute it; TRACE T4b).
- **Note.** Excel error cells (`#N/A` …) probably arrive through COM as large negative whole numbers and would be written as numbers (MEDIUM, cannot be tested here; how to check: put `=NA()` in a mapped cell and run Step 4).

- **What the code does.** A mapping may carry `calculation = {'type': …, 'cell1': …, 'cell2': …}`. The two values are taken from the BARE cell-reference keys of A-R83 (so only cells some mapping already reads are available; an unmapped cell silently counts as 0); empty, `None` or 0 count as 0; a value that is not a plain number → the text `Error`; `subtract` → cell1 − cell2, `add` → +, `multiply` → ×, `divide` → ÷ or the text `Div/0` when dividing by 0; any other type → `Unknown`; results are not rounded. No shipped mapping has a calculation (all `None`), so this never runs. The `workbook` argument is unused.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:130-136` (branch), `:188-218` (`_calculate_value`); documented `power_query_extractor/config/cell_mappings.py:1-27` (format at `:15`).

### 5.12 Category 11 — Consolidated output layout (A-R86 to A-R95)

#### A-R86 — Consolidated workbook file
- **What the code does.** A NEW workbook every run, written with openpyxl (no Excel needed), named `PQ_Extraction_Report_<%Y%m%d_%H%M>.xlsx` (year first, unlike every other name in the program, A-R33), saved in the `Annual Statement-…` folder chosen by the LAST Step 4 scan (`self.level1_folder`, A-R42) — a scan during a run therefore redirects the output (RAN PQ gui_probe). It holds only this run's clients; nothing is merged with earlier runs; a second run in the same minute overwrites it; it is saved directly (no staging). Any error (bad sheet name, folder gone, file open in Excel) is logged and re-raised; the worker then shows `Error` / `Processing failed: <e>` and the run's extracted figures exist only in the log (on screen and in the log file). The folder is not created if missing (error).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:174-186` (name `:175-176`, path `:177`, save `:179`, error `:184-186`); folder `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:632` (set by scan), `:945` (used); error dialog `:957-960`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T4 Part D; me s1: `PQ_Extraction_Report_20261021_0640.xlsx`).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); `data_consolidator.py` is unchanged in A since then.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R87 — Sheet layout
- **What the code does.**
  - Sheet order: `Summary` first (A-R91, inserted at position 0 after the others are built), then one sheet per output group in configuration order (`GSTR-3B Difference`, `GSTR-1 Difference`, `Turnover GSTR-1`, `Reco Diff`, `Table 6J Match`, `Table 8D Limits`, `IMS Reco`, `Extra In Purchase`, `Extra In GSTR2B`).
  - Each data sheet: A1 `<sheet name> Report` bold 14, merged A1:C1; row 2 empty; row 3 headers `Client Name`, `Successfully Run`, then each output column in configuration order — bold white (FFFFFF) size 12 on solid blue `0078D4`, centred, thin borders; from row 4 one row per processed client, in processing order.
  - Column A = the client FOLDER name (the Level-2 name of A-R35, e.g. `Beta Demo Industries Pvt Ltd-GJ`), with a border.
  - Column B = `✓` in bold green `107C10` when that report's status is success, else `✗` in bold orange-red `D83B01` — so ✓ reflects only the refresh status (A-R73), not whether figures were found.
  - Columns C onward: the values of A-R88 to A-R90, each with a border.
  - Column width = min(longest text in the column + 2, 50) (empty and zero cells ignored; the merged title counts for column A).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:27-49` (workbook, styles `:34-42`, loop `:45`), `:66-71` (title), `:86-99` (headers), `:104-121` (client and ✓/✗), `:166-172` (next row, widths, Summary), `:416-432` (`_autofit_columns`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T4 Part D; me s1).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R88 — Which extracted value goes into a column · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** For each configured column of a report whose status is success and that has no calculation:
  1. walk the stored values in the order they were stored (sheets in `CELL_MAPPINGS` order, inside a sheet the field name then the cell reference) and take the FIRST key that CONTAINS the column title as a piece of text;
  2. only if that gave nothing (`None`) look up the exact key `"<sheet name>_<column title>"` with spaces → `_`.
  Column titles with a space (`Diff IGST`, `IGST Diff`, `Table 6J Match`, …) can never be contained in the underscore field names, so they always fall through to step 2 and show the configured cell. The one-word titles `IGST`, `CGST`, `SGST` of `IMS Reco`, `Extra In Purchase` and `Extra In GSTR2B` are contained in `GSTR-3B_Difference_Diff_IGST` etc., which are stored first — so those nine columns show J15/K15/L15 (RAN: 13 of 22 columns as configured, 9 cross-wired). This is a technical mismatch between configuration and code; which cell is right is the author's call (see the warning box in 5.11).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:137-150` (substring loop `:142-145`, exact key `:147-150`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s1; TRACE T4 Part E; XL E3; registry check2.py).
- **History.** The lookup is unchanged since `edac6a3`; the affected one-word columns arrived with `47a1492` (both shared - before the split on 30-10-2025), so the cross-wiring exists in A and B alike.

#### A-R89 — When a figure shows as "Missing" · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** The missing text is `EXTRACTION_OPTIONS['missing_data_text']` = `Missing` (fallback `Missing` in the code too). It is written:
  - in EVERY column of a report whose refresh status is not success — even when values were read (the log meanwhile says `(Data extracted despite error)`), e.g. after an error dialog, a failed `Info!BB2` check, "Not selected for processing", "No ITC report found" or "pywin32 is not installed…";
  - for a value that is `None` or an empty text (missing sheet, empty cell, cell that could not be read).
  The number 0 is kept as 0.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:128-129` (status not success), `:152-154` (empty value); text `power_query_extractor/config/cell_mappings.py:252`; log `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:924-925, 935-936`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T4 Part D: a failed ITC with complete data showed ✗ and Missing everywhere).

#### A-R90 — Number display · [GST RULE]
- **What the code does.** A value that is a whole or decimal number and is not 0 gets the cell format `#,##0.00` (two decimals, thousands separators); the stored value keeps full precision (no rounding). `True` counts as a number (Python treats it as 1) and gets the format; 0 and `False` keep the general format; a number stored as TEXT in the report (e.g. `'1,234.00'`) is copied as text with no format, so Excel will not add it up (RAN TRACE T4 obs 4).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/data_consolidator.py:160-162`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T4 Part D).
- **Note.** Tagged [GST RULE] because it is display rounding of figures; nothing is judged.

#### A-R91 — Consolidated Summary sheet
- **What the code does.** Sheet `Summary` at position 0: A1 `Extraction Summary` bold 16; A3 `Generated: <%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S>`; A4 `Total Clients: <n>`; A5 `ITC Reports Processed: <x>/<n>`; A6 `Sales Reports Processed: <y>/<n>`, where x and y count reports whose status is success and n is EVERY client of the run (a Sales-only client counts as an ITC non-success); A8 `Sheets Created:` bold; from A9 one line `• <sheet name>` per output group. No column widths are set.

### 5.13 Category 12 — Excel handling and safety (A-R96 to A-R108)

#### A-R96 — Excel or openpyxl for Step 3 reports
- **What the code does.** When `core/excel_handler.py` is loaded: if `platform.system() == 'Windows'` AND both `import win32com.client` and `import pythoncom` succeed → `USE_WIN32COM = True` (INFO `Using win32com for Excel operations (Power Query support)`), else a WARNING `win32com not available. Install with: pip install pywin32` (Windows only). Reports are then built through Excel (A-R97) or, otherwise, with openpyxl, logging WARNING `Not on Windows - Power Query may not be preserved!` (also printed on Windows without pywin32) and `Using openpyxl - Power Query content may be lost!`. openpyxl saving drops the Power Query part (`customXml/item*.xml` with `DataMashup`) and macros, and the run still reports success (RAN XL E1, CORE exp5, EXT t6). No check for a DataMashup part exists. On Windows, start-up refuses to run without pywin32 (A-R124), so in normal office use the COM path runs (MEDIUM — it depends on how the app is started).
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:19-28` (switch), `:89-102` (choice; warning `:98`), `:267` (warning).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (CORE exp5; XL E1; TRACE T6 log: the two warnings per report).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R97 — Step 3 Excel session (per report)
- **What the code does.**
  1. `pythoncom.CoInitialize()`;
  2. `GetActiveObject("Excel.Application")` FIRST — i.e. the user's own running Excel (INFO `Using existing Excel instance`) — else `Dispatch("Excel.Application")` (INFO `Created new Excel instance`; `Dispatch` itself may attach to a running Excel — MEDIUM);
  3. `Visible = False`, `DisplayAlerts = False`, `ScreenUpdating = False` (applied to the user's Excel too, hiding it);
  4. staging copy (A-R98), open (A-R101), find the sheet (A-R58), write (A-R56), `Save()`, `Close(SaveChanges=True)`, 0.5 s, copy to the Version folder;
  5. `finally`: if a workbook is still open, `Close(SaveChanges=False)` (errors ignored); then `ScreenUpdating = True` and `DisplayAlerts = True` OUTSIDE any protection (if Excel has died these raise and the rest of the clean-up is skipped — RAN with fake objects, CORE exp6); then `Quit()` ALWAYS, because the "Don't quit if Excel was already open" test looks at `self.excel_app`, which is never set (so the user's own Excel is quit too, with alerts switched back on just before); `CoUninitialize()` (errors ignored) while local variables still hold Excel objects; delete the staging file.
  `Visible` is never restored. One Excel start/attach and quit per report, two per client.
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:56` (`excel_app = None`, never changed), `:104-260` (CoInitialize `:120`; attach `:123-128`; settings `:131-133`; save/close `:201-210`; copy `:215`; finally `:230-260`; the unprotected lines `:239-240`; the test `:242`; Quit `:244`; CoUninitialize `:249-252`); `__del__` `:58-64` never acts.
- **Evidence.** READ (cannot run COM here); control flow RAN with fake COM objects (CORE exp6: the fake "user" Excel was quit; with a dead Excel the finally block raised and the staging file stayed).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.** (Section 9 compares this with the author's Standard 12.)

#### A-R98 — Local staging of Step 3 reports
- **What the code does.** When the main window starts, the folder `%TEMP%\gst_excel_temp` (`tempfile.gettempdir()` + `gst_excel_temp`) is created; it is never removed. For each report: a stale file of the report's name there is deleted (errors ignored); the template is copied there under the REPORT's file name with `shutil.copy2`; Excel (or openpyxl) works on that local copy; the result is copied with `shutil.copy2` to the final path in the Version folder (an existing file there is overwritten without warning; a copy broken half-way leaves a partial file and returns failure); the staging file is deleted (best effort; it is left behind when the COM clean-up raises, A-R97). If `%TEMP%` is unusable, the folder creation raises and the window cannot start. Two copies of the program building the same report name in the same minute would share a staging file (LOW).
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:53-54` (folder), `:147-155` / `:273-279` (stale delete and copy), `:212-215` / `:305-308` (final copy), `:254-260` / `:315-322` (delete); created at `gui/main_window.py:116`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (EXT t6, me s4: `gst_excel_temp` created in the scratch TEMP; staging files removed after each report).
- **History.** Local staging since `12d505f` (A only (after the split)); at the split the template was copied straight to the output path and edited there (`core/excel_handler.py:135@47a1492`, `:239@47a1492`); the `gst_excel_temp` folder itself since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R99 — A Step 3 report counts as created if the output file exists
- **What the code does.** COM path: after the final copy, `output_path.exists()` → True (size logged, not checked), else ERROR `Output file not found!` → False. openpyxl path: returns `output_path.exists()`. Content is not checked (A-R58: a report with no Links sheet still "succeeds").
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:217-224` (COM), `:310` (openpyxl).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (openpyxl path, TRACE T6).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R100 — "Already open" detection
- **What the code does.** Before opening a file, both Excel users ask the Excel they hold for its open workbooks and reuse the first whose `FullName`, made absolute, equals the file's absolute path (`Path` equality — case-insensitive on Windows). A drive-letter path and a UNC path to the same file do not match; a workbook open in ANOTHER Excel process is not seen; a OneDrive/SharePoint workbook (web address) never matches. Errors while looping are not caught here. Step 3's staging file is normally never open, so the branch matters mainly for Step 4 (MEDIUM).
- **Where.** `utils/excel_com.py:19-29`; used `core/excel_handler.py:337-340`, `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:709-712`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (tests `tests/test_excel_com.py` pass, PQ).
- **History.** Shared helper since `ba2a753` (A only (after the split)); before, the same loop was typed in both places.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R101 — Step 3 open with one fallback
- **What the code does.** Missing file → `FileNotFoundError`. "Already open" → reuse (A-R100). Then `Workbooks.Open(<path>)` with no options. If that fails and the error text contains `-2147352567` (a general "exception occurred" code — MEDIUM on the meaning) or the word `path` (any case), or the path is longer than 218 characters, the 8.3 short path is tried (Windows `GetShortPathName`; skipped if it equals the long path, as it does where short names are switched off); otherwise — or if that also fails — the FIRST error is raised again. No `IgnoreReadOnlyRecommended`, no `UpdateLinks`, no read-only check.
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:324-369` (conditions `:355-356`, short path `:359-366`, re-raise `:369`); `utils/helpers.py:580-590` (`get_short_path`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN with fake objects (CORE exp6: the first error was re-raised).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R102 — Step 4 open with several fallbacks
- **What the code does.**
  1. Missing file → log `❌ File does not exist` and `FileNotFoundError`.
  2. Read 1 byte as an access test (failure → log and raise).
  3. Already open in this Excel → reuse (`📌 File already open, using existing workbook`).
  4. If the path is longer than **218** characters ("Excel's typical limit"): `/` → `\`, then prefix `\\?\` (local) or `\\?\UNC\` (network) — Windows' extended-length form; whether Excel accepts it is not sure (LOW).
  5. Attempt 1: `Workbooks.Open(<path>)`.
  6. Attempt 2: close WITHOUT SAVING every workbook in that Excel whose file NAME (not full path) equals this file's name, wait 3 s, open again (in a borrowed user Excel a different workbook with the same name would lose its unsaved changes — MEDIUM).
  7. Attempt 3: wait 2 s; try the 8.3 short path if different (any error there, even a failed open, is reported only as `Could not get short path`); then, if the extended prefix was added, open the plain path; otherwise `Open(Filename=<path>, UpdateLinks=0, ReadOnly=False, IgnoreReadOnlyRecommended=True, Notify=False)`.
  8. All failed → `❌ All attempts failed. Final error: …`; for `-2147352567` a list of likely causes (file open in another program, corrupted, path too long, OneDrive/SharePoint sync) and, over 218 characters, `📋 Path length is definitely an issue!` / `Consider enabling long path support in Windows`; the error is raised.
  `workbook.ReadOnly` is never checked after opening.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:687-799` (missing `:692-694`; access `:697-703`; reuse `:709-712`; prefix `:720-732`; attempt 1 `:735-741`; attempt 2 `:744-753`; attempt 3 `:756-783`; failure `:784-799`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN with a fake Excel (PQ: a 232-character path → two `\\?\` opens, then the plain path, then the error re-raised).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R103 — Step 4 Excel sessions
- **What the code does.** Two separate sessions per report, both with `Dispatch` (not a private `DispatchEx`): the refresh session is visible and maximised (A-R71) and saves; the extraction session is hidden, alerts off, closes without saving and quits. Each starts with `CoInitialize` and ends in `finally` with `Close`, `Quit` and `CoUninitialize`, every error swallowed; `CoUninitialize` runs while the local variables still hold the Excel objects. Four Excel starts/attaches per client with both reports. If `Dispatch` attaches to the user's own Excel, that Excel is maximised, typed into and then quit with alerts off (unsaved user work may be closed without saving — MEDIUM, not testable here). Step 3 (`GetActiveObject`/`Dispatch`) and Step 4 (`Dispatch`) can end up driving the same Excel when run at the same time (MEDIUM).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:215-218, 394-415` (refresh), `:533-539, 618-636` (extraction).
- **Evidence.** READ only.
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R104 — The 218-character path threshold · ⚠
- **What the code does.** The figure 218 ("Excel's typical limit") is used only to choose open fallbacks (A-R101, A-R102) and to explain errors, and is quoted in the folder-name comment and two help texts. Nothing measures a path BEFORE folders and files are created; there is no path-length guard (the `7347383` commit message itself names such a guard as "the durable fix"). Windows' own classic limit of 260 characters (README) is not used anywhere.
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:356`; `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:720, 795`; `utils/constants.py:85-90` (comment); `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:115-116, 122` (help texts).
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **⚠ (added here, not in the registry).** The limit is Excel behaviour and can differ between Excel versions and Windows long-path settings; the author should confirm the figure.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R105 — Error-dialog handling · ⚠
- **What the code does.**
  - **Finding** (searched over ALL top-level windows on the desktop, not only this Excel's): a window counts if it is visible and enabled, its title (lower-case) contains any of `microsoft excel`, `error`, `problem`, `cannot`, `failed`, `unable`, `could not`, `exception`, `invalid`, `missing`, and its window class is `#32770`, `NUIDialog` or `bosa_sdm_msword`; the first such window is taken. (`bosa_sdm_msword` is, by its name, a Word dialog class; whether Excel uses another one is not sure — LOW; how to check: `win32gui.GetClassName` on a live Excel dialog.)
  - **Reading:** the title plus the text of child controls whose class contains `static` or `text` and whose text is longer than 5 characters, whitespace collapsed, first 200 characters; fallback `Power Query error occurred`. This becomes the report's error text, the log line and (via A-R89) a "Missing" row.
  - **Dismissing:** click (`BM_CLICK`) the first child button whose text contains `ok`, `close`, `yes` or `continue` (plain substring); if none, send `WM_CLOSE`; then 0.5 s. Another program's dialog (even this app's own Tk error box, which on Windows is a `#32770` dialog titled e.g. "Error") can be clicked, "Yes" included (MEDIUM-HIGH).
  - **When:** 5 checks 1 s apart after the refresh wait (stop at the first found), then once more after the extended wait if none was found.
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:305-342` (checks), `:417-453` (`_find_error_window`: words `:425-436`, classes `:441`), `:455-490` (`_get_window_text`: `> 5` `:469`, `[:200]` `:484`), `:492-525` (`_close_error_window`: words `:506`, click `:508`, close `:518-520`, pause `:522`).
- **Evidence.** READ only.
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **⚠ (added here, not in the registry).** The title words, button words and window classes assume an English Excel/Windows of a certain version.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R106 — openpyxl load options for templates
- **What the code does.** `load_workbook(<staging copy>, data_only=False, keep_links=True)`; `keep_vba` is not set, so macros of `.xlsm`/`.xltm` templates are dropped. (Different from the dead `EXCEL_SAFETY['save_options']` of A-R107, which says `keep_links False`.)

#### A-R107 — `EXCEL_SAFETY` · DEAD
- **What the code does.** `max_retries 3`, `retry_delay 1` (seconds), `temp_prefix 'temp_gst_'`, `backup_prefix 'backup_'`, `verification_size_min 10240` (10 KB), `save_options {data_only: False, keep_vba: False, keep_links: False}`. Stored as `ExcelHandler.safety_config` and never read. Real behaviour differs on every value: no save retries; staging files carry the report name with no prefix (A-R98); backups use the infix `_backup_` (A-R46); the only size check compares source and copy sizes, and the scan's minimum is 1024 bytes (A-R9); openpyxl loads with `keep_links=True` (A-R106).

#### A-R108 — All Python warnings switched off
- **What the code does.** `warnings.filterwarnings('ignore')` runs the moment `core/excel_handler.py` is loaded (and that happens when ANY `core` module is imported, through `core/__init__.py`), so every Python warning in the whole program is hidden from then on — openpyxl's warnings about unsupported workbook features and deprecation notices included. (Python warnings are different from the program's own log warnings, which still appear.)

### 5.14 Category 13 — Performance and operational constants (A-R109 to A-R113)

#### A-R109 — One directory listing per scan, reusing its file details
- **What the code does.** The source folder is listed ONCE with `os.scandir`; each entry's cached details (size, type, dates) are reused for the checks of A-R8/A-R9 and for the file record (`get_file_info(path, stat)`), so the only per-file network read left is the 8-byte signature (A-R10). The code comments give the motivation: on the office share each extra existence check or `stat` was a separate round-trip (~45 ms for a Setup check, ~270 ms per file open over the VPN). A listing error throws away everything already listed and returns an empty list, which the screen then reports as "No valid GST files found".
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:309-344` (listing `:328`, cached stat `:334`), `:363-365` (error → `[]`); `core/file_parser.py:158, 188`; `utils/helpers.py:129-166` (`get_file_info` with the cached stat).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T8 §6: a missing folder inside the scan helper returns `[]` with only a log line).
- **History.** `a603c02` (single listing), `56aaad9` (reuse of the cached details) (both A only (after the split)); at the split three globs plus a re-validation per file.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R110 — Parallel signature reads
- **What the code does.** At most **16** signature reads at once (`_SIGNATURE_READ_WORKERS`), and only when there are **4** or more candidates (`_MIN_FILES_FOR_PARALLEL`); below that, one after another. Results are collected in order and then sorted, so the output is the same either way. The worker threads only read files. The commit message reports 2508 files in about 41 s on the office share instead of an estimated 11 minutes.
- **Where.** `utils/helpers.py:267-272` (constants and comment), `:346-361` (pool, `min(16, n)` at `:352`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T8 §6: 3 files sequential and 5 files in the pool gave the same sorted order).
- **History.** `4f84ba9` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R111 — The Level-1 folder is worked out once per run
- **What the code does.** The run folder (A-R32 / A-R43) is computed on the first client and reused for all later clients of the run (the code comment: re-scanning the target for every client was a redundant network round-trip). A listing error on the first client fails that client; the next client tries again.
- **Where.** `core/file_organizer.py:45-47` (`_level1_folder = None`), `:150-161` (`_get_level1_folder`).
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **History.** `275d9a4` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R112 — Fixed pauses in Step 3 Excel work
- **What the code does.** `time.sleep(0.5)` after the staging copy ("Give Windows time to complete the copy" — `shutil.copy2` has already finished when it returns, so the pause is not needed for correctness, MEDIUM) and `time.sleep(0.5)` after closing the workbook: 1 s per report through COM, 2 s per client. The openpyxl path has no pauses.
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:155, 210`.
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R113 — Setup re-check delay and memory
- **What the code does.** Any change to one of the four path boxes schedules a re-check of the checklist **350 ms** later (`REVALIDATE_DELAY_MS`, comment "debounce: avoid stat'ing a UNC path on every keystroke"); a new change cancels and re-schedules it. Each exact path text's "exists / does not exist" answer is remembered and forgotten only on a FORCED refresh (when the Setup tab is built and after every dark-mode switch). So a network share that drops and comes back keeps its old ✕ / ✓ until then (READ, HIGH).
- **Where.** `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:135` (350 ms), `:506-519` (traces and scheduling), `:521-529` (memory), `:531-535` (forced clear); forced `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:156`, `gui/main_window.py:188-194`.
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **History.** `322717f` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

### 5.15 Category 14 — Settings, cache, logs and run history (A-R114 to A-R123)

#### A-R114 — Settings file location and format
- **What the code does.** One file, `Path.home() / '.gst_organizer_cache.json'` (on Windows `%USERPROFILE%\.gst_organizer_cache.json`), holding one line of JSON (non-ASCII characters escaped, so any reader encoding works). Every save first READS the file (to keep `recent_folders`, A-R117) and then REWRITES the whole file in place (no temporary file; a crash mid-write leaves broken JSON). A load error gives empty settings with only an ERROR log line `Error loading cache: …` (nothing on screen). RAN (me s4): a broken file loads as `{}` and the next save writes a fresh file; a file holding valid JSON that is NOT an object makes every later save fail (`Error saving cache: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'`), and if that value is non-empty (`[1]`, `5`) the main window cannot even be built (`AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'`, raised at start-up — the program stops with "Critical error"); `[]` is treated as "no settings". The path is typed out in three places.
- **Where.** `gui/utils/cache_manager.py:14-48` (path `:15`, load `:18-27`, save `:29-48`, write `:45-46`); `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:41`; `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:118`; start-up restore `gui/main_window.py:120-121, 373-389`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s4; EXT t8).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); the extractor readers point at this file since `c1583d6` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R115 — Settings keys and defaults on restore
- **What the code does.**

| Key | Written from | Default on restore | Restored into |
|---|---|---|---|
| `source_folder` | Step 1 box | `''` | Step 1 box |
| `itc_template` | Step 1 box | `''` | Step 1 box |
| `sales_template` | Step 1 box | `''` | Step 1 box |
| `target_folder` | Step 1 box | `''` | Step 1 box (and, through it, Step 4's box, A-R144) |
| `processing_mode` | radio | `'fresh'` | radio |
| `workflow_mode` | radio | `'organize'` | radio |
| `include_client_name` | tick | `False` | tick |
| `dark_mode` | title-strip tick | `False` | tick; if `True`, dark mode is applied at start-up (which saves the file again) |
| `client_name_max_length` | number box | `35`; 0 or less → 35 | number box |
| `recent_folders` | added by the cache manager | — | never read (A-R117) |

  Values are not validated on restore (an unknown mode text is restored as it is and only fails later, A-R43).

#### A-R116 — When settings are saved
- **What the code does.** Saved after: a Browse of any of the four paths; a workflow radio change; an arrow click on the length box; the global "Include client name" tick (both on and off); a dark-mode switch (also at start-up when the saved setting is dark). NOT saved: a path typed into a box, a processing-mode radio change (the radios have no command), the per-client "Name" toggles (never stored at all), the Step 4 options (A-R119), and closing the window. If the length box holds non-numeric text, `IntVar.get()` raises inside the save and that save is lost (RAN me s4 for the `TclError`; GUI exp5).

#### A-R117 — `recent_folders` · DEAD (write-only)

- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).

#### A-R118 — How the standalone extractor finds its folder
- **What the code does.** `launch_extractor.py` → `PowerQueryExtractor()` with no folder → it tries, in order: the home settings file of A-R114, then three files RELATIVE TO THE CURRENT FOLDER — `gst_organizer_cache.json`, `.cache/app_state.json`, `config/last_session.json` — and in each the first non-empty of the keys `target_folder`, `target_path`, `output_folder`, `last_target`; nothing found → `''`. The folder found is not checked for existence. The standalone window then scans it at once (on the window's thread). If nothing was found, the panel itself reads `target_folder` of the home file once more. Nothing in A writes the three fallback files or the three extra keys. Embedded Step 4 never reads the settings file (its folder comes from Step 1, A-R144).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:27-34, 36-63` (list `:38-45`, keys `:55`); `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:118, 135-139, 587-599`; `launch_extractor.py:14-18`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (EXT t8: with the home file blank, an `output_folder` in a current-folder file was used; PQ).
- **History.** Home file FIRST since `c1583d6` (A only (after the split)); at the split both readers used `Path("gst_organizer_cache.json")` in the current folder (`power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:39@47a1492`, `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:28@47a1492`), so the extractor could not find the main program's settings.
- **Older documents.** `PROJECT_AUDIT_ANNUALSTATEMENT.md:411, 449-451` still describes that mismatch as current.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R119 — Step 4 options are not stored
- **What the code does.** The wait time (`10`), the refreshed-file suffix (`_Refreshed_{timestamp}`) and the Skip Refresh tick (off) start at these defaults at every launch, in both the embedded and the standalone window; nothing writes them anywhere. After a restart, copies made with a custom suffix are recognised only through the legacy `_refreshed_` marker (A-R68).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:270, 297, 315`.
- **Evidence.** READ; search: no write of these values.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R120 — Main log file
- **What the code does.** The moment `main.py` is loaded: `logging.basicConfig(level=INFO, format='%(asctime)s - %(name)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s', handlers=[FileHandler('gst_organizer.log'), StreamHandler(sys.stdout)])`. The file name has no folder, so it is created in the CURRENT folder (whatever folder the program was started from), appended to, never rotated or trimmed, written in the PC's default text encoding — on a typical Windows code page (cp1252) lines with emoji (`✓ ❌ ⚠️ 🏢 …`) fail with `--- Logging error ---` and are missing from the file (RAN EXT t15, simulated). If the current folder is not writable, start-up fails before any message. The console copy uses the UTF-8 wrapper of A-R127. Embedded Step 4 also logs here (A-R121).
- **Where.** `main.py:20-27`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (EXT t11: the log appeared in the scratch current folder, not the program folder; EXT t15).
- **History.** Since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025). A real `gst_organizer.log` (a program log, described by kind only) was committed with the code: it is present in the trees of `edac6a3` and `6237597`, was deleted by `cbfa4a5`, and is gone after the merge `9c56a1c` (all shared - before the split on 30-10-2025) — which shows that the log, written into the current folder, ends up wherever the program is started from, the repository included.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R121 — Extractor log file
- **What the code does.** The same set-up with `FileHandler('pq_extractor.log')` (current folder) and a console handler on standard error, done when `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py` is loaded — i.e. only for the standalone launcher. Embedded Step 4 deliberately imports `extractor_window` directly so this set-up does not run and its lines go to `gst_organizer.log`. (`logging.basicConfig` does nothing if logging is already configured.) The pywin32 warning of A-R64 is emitted BEFORE this set-up and therefore does not reach `pq_extractor.log` (RAN PQ).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/extractor_main.py:12-19`; `gui/tabs/extract_tab.py:8-10, 24-25`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (PQ: `pq_extractor.log` created in the current folder; me — a `pq_extractor.log` sits in this task's scratch work folder from the earlier run).
- **Older documents.** The design note (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-merge-extractor-design.md:54-56`) says this set-up "moves into the standalone entry point"; it did not move (DOCS P12).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R122 — What is logged and at which level
- **What the code does.** INFO lines (all written to the log file) carry client names, folder paths, file names, every Links value (first 50 characters), every copied file, and in Step 4 every extracted figure (twice: once per cell as it is read, once in the "Extraction summary"). `ReportProcessor._log` ALWAYS logs at INFO and sends the line to the Step 4 window with the plain `info` colour, whatever happened — so Step 4 error lines (`❌ …`) look like ordinary lines; lines logged only with `logger.warning` / `logger.error` (e.g. "Could not activate Excel window", "Sheet '…' not found") never reach the window. The Step 3 worker writes mostly to the on-screen log and only exceptions to the file.
- **Where.** `core/excel_handler.py:153, 158, 195, 214, 220`; `utils/helpers.py:210, 229`; `core/file_organizer.py:292`; `core/file_parser.py:131`; `power_query_extractor/core/report_processor.py:53-60` (`_log`), `:590, 603-611` (figures); `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:174, 406, 524`.
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **Note.** Because client names and figures go into a file in the current folder, the log is client data (see Section 9 / the confidentiality appendix).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R123 — No run history
- **What the code does.** There is no history file or database. The only records of a run are the Step 3 summary workbook (A-R61), the organisation text reports (A-R62), the consolidated workbook (A-R86) and the log files (A-R120, A-R121).
- **Where.** Absence (READ; EXT §4.1).
- **Evidence.** READ (no code writes any file other than those listed above).
- **Not a GST rule.**

### 5.16 Category 15 — Start-up and dependencies (A-R124 to A-R130)

#### A-R124 — Which packages are required
- **What the code does.** Two requirements are checked before the window is built:

| Module checked | Package | Purpose text | Essential? | Consequence text |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `openpyxl` | `openpyxl` | "Reading and writing Excel files" | always | — |
| `pythoncom` | `pywin32` | "Excel automation: preserves Power Query when creating reports, and powers the refresh in Step 4" | only when `sys.platform == 'win32'` | "Reports would be created WITHOUT their Power Query connections, and Step 4 could not refresh them." |

  Presence = already loaded, or `importlib.util.find_spec` finds the module file (it is NOT imported, so a pywin32 that exists but cannot load passes — RAN XL E2, simulated). `lxml` and `tkinter` are not checked. The console report shows one line per package, `[OK     ]`, `[MISSING]` (essential) or `[not found]` (optional), plus explanations and `Install with:  pip install <package>` and the running interpreter. Essential missing (after the install offer, A-R125) → ERROR `Required package missing: <package>`, wait for Enter, exit code 1; optional missing → WARNING `<package> not installed - <purpose>` and the program continues.
- **Where.** `utils/requirements_check.py:17` (`ON_WINDOWS`), `:37-61` (`REQUIREMENTS`, `essential=ON_WINDOWS` at `:57`), `:64-70` (`_is_installed`), `:73-84`, `:93-133` (report); `main.py:68-106`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (START; XL E2).
- **History.** Check added in `f363946`; pywin32 essential on Windows since `be6418c` (both A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R125 — Offer to install
- **What the code does.** If anything (essential OR optional) is missing, a hidden Tk root shows a Yes/No box `Install missing packages?` listing `• <package> - <purpose>` (+ consequence) and the running interpreter. Yes → a 760×380 window `Installing…` shows the output of `<this python> -m pip install -r <program folder>/requirements.txt` (the WHOLE file — pytest included — when that file exists; otherwise the package names), killed after **600 s** (`INSTALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`); success → start a new copy of the program with `[sys.executable] + sys.argv` and exit 0 (if the restart fails: "Could not restart automatically. Please start the app again."); failure → box `Installation failed` with the manual command (paths not quoted) and, for an essential package, `The app cannot start without them.` Off Windows pywin32 stays "missing", so the offer comes back at every start (RAN START).
- **Where.** `main.py:78-95`; `utils/dependency_installer.py:23` (600 s), `:26-38` (command), `:41-60` (run), `:63-74` (restart), `:77-85` (text), `:88-158` (dialog; window size `:123`; whole file `:133-134`; manual command `:142`).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (START, dialogs replaced: command `[sys.executable, -m, pip, install, -r, <app>/requirements.txt]`).
- **History.** `a5ad487` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R126 — `requirements.txt` · ⚠
- **What the code does.** Comment "Python 3.7 or higher required"; `openpyxl>=3.1.2`; `pywin32>=305; sys_platform == 'win32'`; `lxml>=4.9.0` ("Optional but recommended for better performance" — nothing in A imports lxml directly); `pytest>=7.0.0` ("Testing"); `black>=23.0.0` and `pylint>=2.17.0` commented out. Minimum versions only, no exact versions.
- **Where.** `requirements.txt:1-19`.
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **History.** pywin32 line added in `064c634`; pytest un-commented in `6c1e1c6` (both A only (after the split)); openpyxl and lxml since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).
- **Older documents.** `README.md:104-108` lists `openpyxl>=3.0.0` (EXT §3.8).
- **⚠** Package versions.
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R127 — A console is assumed
- **What the code does.** At load time `sys.stdout` is replaced by a UTF-8 wrapper of `sys.stdout.buffer`; with no console (`pythonw.exe`, where `sys.stdout` is `None`) this raises `AttributeError` before any log exists and the program dies silently (RAN START / EXT t11, simulated). A banner is printed (`🚀 GST File Organizer v3.0 - Production Ready` between two lines of 60 `=`), then the requirements report. `_pause()` waits for Enter and ignores end-of-input, Ctrl+C and operating-system errors (`EOFError`, `KeyboardInterrupt`, `OSError`), but not the `RuntimeError` that `input()` raises when there is no standard input at all (unreachable today because of the first point; EXT §5.2).
- **Where.** `main.py:17` (wrapper), `:45-54` (`_pause`), `:62-73` (banner, report).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (EXT t11).
- **History.** UTF-8 wrapper since `edac6a3` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025); `_pause` since `be6418c` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R128 — Preparing the program folder
- **What the code does.** The program folder (the folder of `main.py`) is put FIRST on Python's search path; the folders `core`, `utils`, `gui`, `logs`, `temp` are created in the program folder (existing ones are fine), and a placeholder `__init__.py` containing `"""Package initialization"""` is written into `core`, `utils`, `gui` if missing. `logs` and `temp` are never used by anything. On a program folder that users may not write into, this stops the program with "Critical error" as long as `logs/` or `temp/` does not exist there yet — they are not in the repository (empty folders, and ignored by `.gitignore:46-47`), so a fresh copy needs write access once; when all five folders and the three `__init__.py` files already exist, `mkdir(exist_ok=True)` normally succeeds without write access (READ, MEDIUM — it depends on the operating system reporting "already exists" before "access denied"; not run on a read-only share; how to check: copy the program to a read-only share with and without `logs/` and `temp/` and start it). [Corrected during verification — originally stated without the "folders not yet present" condition.] The standalone launcher APPENDS its folder to the search path instead (searched last).
- **Where.** `main.py:29-43`; `launch_extractor.py:9-10`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (EXT t11: `logs/` and `temp/` created in the copied program folder).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R129 — Step 4 loading guard
- **What the code does.** Step 4 loads the extractor panel inside a guard that catches ANY error; on failure it logs `Power Query Extractor unavailable: …` and shows, on a pale-orange (`#FFF4E5`) card, `⚠️  Power Query refresh is unavailable` / "This step needs pywin32 (Excel automation), which is not installed for the Python running this app. … Install it with:    pip install pywin32 … Details: <error>", and below it "Steps 1-3 are unaffected: organising files and creating the reports works without pywin32." Because pywin32 is guarded inside the extractor itself (A-R64), the panel loads fine without pywin32, so this card cannot appear for that cause and would blame pywin32 for any other loading error (RAN GUI/DCP §1.5).
- **Where.** `gui/tabs/extract_tab.py:21-30` (guard), `:67-69`, `:84-105` (card).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (GUI: without pywin32 the panel loaded).
- **History.** `c463256` (A only (after the split)).
- **Not a GST rule.**

#### A-R130 — Package version labels · DEAD
- **What the code does.** `__version__ = '3.0.0'` in `core`, `gui`, `utils`; `__version__ = "1.0.0"` in `power_query_extractor`; `__author__ = 'GST File Organizer Team'` in `core`. Never read.
- **Where.** `core/__init__.py:12-13`; `gui/__init__.py:11`; `utils/__init__.py:38`; `power_query_extractor/__init__.py:3`.

### 5.17 Category 16 — User-interface behaviour rules (A-R131 to A-R157)

These rules are presentational; none is a GST rule. Section 10 describes the screens; here only the exact values the code fixes.

#### A-R131 — Main window
- **What the code does.** Title `GST File Organizer v3.0`; size 1200×800; minimum 1000×700; placed in the centre of the screen; ttk theme `clam` (ignored if unavailable); background `#F8F9FA`. Tabs `📁 Step 1: Setup`, `✅ Step 2: Validation`, `🚀 Step 3: Processing`, `🔄 Step 4: Extract`. On every tab change the status bar shows `Step N: <Setup|Validation|Processing|Extract>` (a fifth tab would show `Step 5: Step 5`). The close button asks `Processing Active` / "Processing is still running. Stop and exit?" ONLY while Step 3 runs (Yes → stop flag, window destroyed after 1 s, the daemon worker dies mid-client); a running Step 4 is not checked.

#### A-R132 — Automatic tab switches
- **What the code does.** A scan that finds at least one client → Step 2; Dry Run and Start → Step 3; the end of a "full" Step 3 → Step 4 (A-R154); Step 4 Start → its "Processing" sub-tab.
- **Where.** `gui/handlers/file_handler.py:82-88`; `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:49, 116, 164`; `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:868`.

- **What the code does.** Four rows (source folder, ITC template, Sales template, target folder):

| Row kind | Blank (or spaces only) | Not existing | Existing but wrong suffix | OK |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Folder (source, target) | ○ `Not selected yet` | ✕ `Folder not found` | — | ✓ the path, shortened to `...` + its last 42 characters when longer than 46 |
| Template (ITC, Sales) | ○ `Not selected yet` | ✕ `File not found` | ✕ `Not an Excel template` (suffix not `.xlsx/.xltx/.xltm/.xlsm`) | ✓ the file name only |

  Existence only: a FILE passes as a folder and a FOLDER named `x.xlsx` passes as a template (RAN TRACE T7). Scan and Re-scan are enabled only when the source row is ✓. The pill reads `N of 4 items set` or `Ready - all 4 items set` (green when all four are ✓, amber otherwise). The strip under the buttons reads `Select a valid source folder to scan` (red tint), `Ready to scan, and to process afterwards` (green) or `Ready to scan · still needed for Step 3: <labels>` (amber). Row colours: light ✓ `#107C10` on `#E8F5E8`, ○ `#8A8F98` on `#F1F3F4`, ✕ `#D83B01` on `#FDECEA`; dark ✓ `#7EE787` on `#1B3326`, ○ `#9AA0A6` on `#3A3A3A`, ✕ `#FF8A7A` on `#4A2320`. The "ready for Step 3" state only colours the pill; Start's own gate is A-R51.

Dry Run uses the full processing gate of A-R51 (both templates and target) although it uses none of them.

#### A-R135 — Run control in Step 3
- **What the code does.** Start is ignored while a Step 3 run is active (`is_processing`); during a run the Start button is greyed and Stop is active, restored at the end (also after an error). Stop only sets a flag that is checked before each client (`🛑 Stop requested by user`). The Step 2 and Step 3 buttons are wired with `.bind('<Button-1>')`, so a greyed button still reacts to a click and none can be pressed from the keyboard. The worker is a daemon thread. Nothing blocks Step 4, Scan, Re-scan, Browse or the Step 2 buttons during a Step 3 run, and Step 3 does not check whether Step 4 is running.

A click in the 7th column flips one client (`☑ Yes` / `☐ No`). Turning one client OFF while the global tick is on turns the global tick off with `Global Setting Disabled` / "Global folder name setting has been turned off since you unchecked an individual client." (this change is not saved). Global ON sets every client ON; global OFF changes no client flag. Number-looking client or state texts break these toggles (A-R17; RAN TRACE T7).

#### A-R140 — Step 3 log and completion
- **What the code does.** Each entry `[HH:MM:SS] <message>`, in Consolas 9, white `#FFFFFF` on `#1E1E1E`, coloured by level: success `#4CAF50`, warning `#FF9800`, error `#F44336`, info `#2196F3`, normal `#FFFFFF`; the box is read-only and never cleared. At start-up: `🎉 Application started`, `💡 Ready to organize your GST files!`, `⚠️ IMPORTANT: Templates must have 'Links' sheet for reports to work!`. During a run: per client the folder levels, `✓ Organized N files`, one line per SUCCESSFUL copy only (failed copies are not listed; see UNREGISTERED U1 for its wording), the report lines of A-R60 (in the Sales part 8 lines per client begin `[DEBUG]` on a normal success, out of 14 different `[DEBUG]` texts in the code), `✅ Completed <client>`; at the end `🎉 ════════ PROCESSING COMPLETE ════════`, `✅ Processed: p/t clients`, `📁 Files organized: N`, `📊 Reports created: N`, `📂 Output location: <Level-1 folder>`, `⏱️ Total processing time: M minutes S seconds`. Then a box `Processing Complete` / "Successfully processed p/t clients … Files organized in: <target>" (also after Stop), the Level-1 folder is opened (`os.startfile` on Windows, `open` on macOS, `xdg-open` elsewhere) and `📂 Opened output folder` is logged (also when the opener failed silently, RAN on Linux).

#### A-R141 — Progress and ETA
- **What the code does.** The bar (0-100) = client index ÷ number of clients × 100; within a client, copying fills the first half of its share (`+ files done ÷ files × 50 ÷ clients`) and report creation starts at the second half (`+ 50 ÷ clients`); the summary step shows 95 and the end 100. Labels: `📊 Progress: X.X%` and the current operation, e.g. `Client i of n: <client> | ETA: <eta>`, `Client i/n: Creating folders | ETA: …`, `Client i/n: Copying <file> | ETA: …`, `Client i/n: Creating Excel reports | ETA: …`, `Creating summary report | ETA: …`, `Processing complete | Total time: Mm Ss`. ETA: `Calculating...` for the first client, then remaining ÷ (done ÷ elapsed) shown as `Ns`, `Mm Ss` or `Hh Mm`; the tracker advances by 0 for the first client and 1 for each later one, so it is always one client behind and ends at total − 1 (RAN me s3: 3 clients → final count 2 of 3), and the summary step therefore shows a non-zero ETA; with one client it shows `Unknown`.
- **Where.** `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:203, 235-241, 251-252, 266-269, 287-289, 457-459, 478-482`; `utils/helpers.py:476-523`; `gui/utils/ui_helpers.py:35-40`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (me s3: `format_duration` 59.9 → `59s`, 61 → `1m 1s`, 3661 → `1h 1m`, 90061 → `25h 1m`).

#### A-R142 — Exported client list
- **What the code does.** Nothing ticked → `No Selection` / `Please select clients to export`. Suggested name `selected_clients_<%Y%m%d_%H%M%S>.txt` (filters "Text files" `*.txt`, "All files"); content: `Selected Clients List`, `Generated: <%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S>`, `Total: N clients`, 50 `=` signs, a blank line, then `<client> - <state>` per ticked client (as written); written in the PC's default encoding (a client name outside that code page fails with `Export Error`); then `Export Complete` / "Exported N clients to: <file name>" and the file is opened with `os.startfile` (Windows only — elsewhere an `Export Error` follows the success box, RAN GUI exp8).
- **Where.** `gui/handlers/client_handler.py:225-257`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (GUI exp8).

#### A-R143 — Step 4 panel behaviour
- **What the code does.** `🚀 Start Processing` (blue `#0078D4`, white, Segoe UI 11 bold) is greyed when the panel is created and when a run starts, and enabled again by ANY scan that finds clients — even during a run (RAN PQ gui_probe) — and after a run; a scan that finds no clients does not grey it. There is no Stop button; the worker is a daemon thread. Messages: empty folder box → `No Folder` / `Please select a folder first`; nothing ticked → `No Selection` / `Please select at least one report to process`; at the end `Complete` / "Processing complete!\n\nOpen the consolidated report?" → Yes → `os.startfile`; a fatal error → `Error` / `Processing failed: <e>`. The status variable's texts (`Ready`, `Scanning folder...`, `No processed folders found`, `Found N clients`, `Error scanning folder`, `Processing <client>`, `Creating consolidated report`, `Processing complete`) are shown in a status bar only by the standalone window (grey `#e0e0e0`, 25 px). Embedded, the scan texts (`Scanning folder...`, `No processed folders found`, `Found N clients`, `Error scanning folder`) are invisible, but the progress texts (`Processing <client>`, `Creating consolidated report`, `Processing complete`, and `Ready` after a run) also appear in the "Processing" sub-tab's progress label, because `_apply_progress` writes the same message there (`power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:1016-1019`) (READ, HIGH) [Corrected during verification — originally stated that all of them are invisible when embedded]. Embedded: no banner, no status bar, no scan at start-up. Standalone: title `Power Query Extractor`, 1000×700, minimum 900×600, theme `clam`, notebook background `#f0f0f0`, tab padding [20, 10], banner `🔄 Power Query Report Extractor` (Segoe UI 18 bold, white on `#0078D4`, 60 px).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:85-95` (styles), `:107-139` (creation), `:147-182` (banner, sub-tabs, status bar), `:328-340` (button), `:573-585` (status bar), `:611-615`, `:756-757` (re-enable), `:845-883` (start), `:968-986` (end), `:1039-1049` (standalone window); `gui/tabs/extract_tab.py:74-80` (embedded flags).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (PQ gui_probe; standalone window started under Xvfb).

#### A-R144 — Step 4 folder follows Step 1
- **What the code does.** The panel is built with Step 1's target (empty at that moment, because settings are restored after the tabs are built) and follows every later change of Step 1's target — the restore at start-up included — as long as the box still holds the value this tab last put there (or is empty); once the user types or browses another folder into the box, Step 1 changes no longer overwrite it. The "full" hand-over after Step 3 (A-R154) sets the box to the target REGARDLESS and does not update this bookkeeping.
- **Where.** `gui/tabs/extract_tab.py:76, 82` (initial), `:108-131` (sync: rule `:124-128`); `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:166-168`; order of construction `gui/main_window.py:53-55`.
- **Evidence.** READ.
- **History.** `bfcb24a` (A only (after the split)).
- **Older documents.** Design note `:85-89` ("following it while the user has not overridden it") — true except for the "full" hand-over (DOCS P18).

#### A-R145 — "Last Refresh Status" text
- **What the code does.** Per client row, for each report type, the newest date modified among files in its Version folder whose names start with `itc_report_` / `sales_report_` and pass A-R68 (using the CURRENT suffix box text), formatted `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M`; shown as `ITC: <t> | Sales: <t>`, `ITC: <t> | Sales: Never`, `ITC: Never | Sales: <t>` or `Never refreshed` (also when the folder cannot be read). Because a refreshed copy keeps the original's date until Excel saves it (A-R67), a failed refresh shows the ORIGINAL report's date as the last refresh (RAN TRACE T10). Recomputed for every row at the end of each run. The header (`Client Name` width 25, `ITC` 8, `Sales` 8, `Last Refresh Status`) does not line up with the rows (name width 30, status width 40).
- **Where.** `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:45-82` (`_latest_refresh_times`), `:410-420` (header), `:690, 709-750` (rows), `:759-808` (refresh, status, suffix, text).
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (TRACE T5, T10).
- **History.** Recomputed after a run since `2acffed`; configured-suffix aware since `2aaa2b1` (both A only (after the split)).

#### A-R146 — Mouse-wheel scrolling
- **What the code does.** Step 1: 22 pixels per scroll unit (`SCROLL_STEP_PX`), 3 units per wheel notch (`SCROLL_UNITS_PER_NOTCH`), fractional movements accumulated, used only when the pointer is over the Setup page and the page overflows, bound application-wide with "add" (never replacing other handlers). Step 4 client list: the same rule with a literal 3. Step 4 log box: `int(-delta/120)` units, so small touch-pad movements scroll nothing. All listen only to `<MouseWheel>` (Windows/macOS); Linux wheels (Button-4/5) do nothing (LOW relevance).
- **Where.** `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:137-138, 191, 193-248`; `power_query_extractor/gui/extractor_window.py:434-478` (literal 3 at `:471`), `:559-565`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (`tests/test_scroll_bindings.py` passes, PQ).
- **History.** `d64d3a9`, `bf2f1dd` (A only (after the split)); log-box wheel `4c42758` (shared - before the split on 30-10-2025).

#### A-R154 — Workflow mode values
- **What the code does.**

| Key | Radio text | Description under it | Tooltip |
|---|---|---|---|
| `organize` (default) | `📁 Organise files & create reports` | `Sort the files and build the ITC/Sales reports, then stop.` | "Sort the files into folders and build the ITC/Sales reports, then stop. / Fast - no Excel automation involved." |
| `full` | `🔄 Full pipeline` | `Also refresh Power Query and extract the data (slower).` | "Everything above, and then open each report in Excel to refresh its Power Query and pull the figures out. / Much slower - Excel is driven for a while per report." |

  Below them: "Step 4 stays available either way - this only decides whether Step 3 continues into it automatically." At the end of every Step 3 run (also after Stop or when all clients failed, as long as no fatal error ended it): `organize` → log `ℹ️ Workflow is 'organise only'. Open Step 4 if you also want to refresh Power Query.`; `full` → log `🔄 Full pipeline: moving to Step 4 to extract…`, then on the window's thread select Step 4, set its folder box to the target (overwriting a folder the user chose there), scan it, log `✅ Step 4 ready - review the client list and press Process to refresh.` (the button is actually called `🚀 Start Processing`); the refresh is NOT started (by design, comment `:139-142`). The "Fast - no Excel automation involved" tooltip is untrue on Windows with pywin32, where Step 3 drives Excel for every report (A-R96, A-R97).
- **Where.** `utils/constants.py:163-172`; default `gui/main_window.py:90`; `gui/tabs/setup_tab.py:95-101` (tooltips), `:316-351` (radios, save); `gui/handlers/processing_handler.py:136-175, 519`.
- **Evidence.** READ; RAN (GUI exp1 "full"; TRACE T6 log line for `organize`).
- **History.** `510f7b4` (A only (after the split)).
